Note that the generated subterms of
M
appear as the last arguments of the
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{i}
-symbols in this rewrite sequence.

5
Observing
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms by their generated subterms

§ 5.1

(overview)
.

In this section we adapt the concepts developed so far for the infinitary λ-calculus to
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
. By combining the rules of
\textit{{R}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\boldsymbol{\triangledown}}
with those of
Reg
and
\textit{{Reg}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, respectively, we obtain the CRSs
\textit{{Reg}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and
\textit{{Reg}}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
for the deconstruction of
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms furnished with an abstraction prefix. We define scope-delimiting and scope
+
-delimiting strategies for
\textit{{Reg}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and
\textit{{Reg}}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
as before by excluding all non-determinism except for sources of
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{0}}
-steps and
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{1}}
-steps.

Definition 5.2

(the CRSs
\textit{{Reg}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and
\textit{{Reg}}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
for decomposing
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms)
.

We extend
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
(see
definition

5.4
) by function symbols
\mathsf{pre}_{n}
with arity one to obtain the signature
\Sigma^{\smash{(λ)}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}=\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}∪\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathsf{pre}_{n}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {n∈ℕ}}}\right\}
. We denote the induced set of (finite) prefixed
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms by
{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ_{\mathsf{letrec}})}}}}\right)
and we adopt the same informal notation for
\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ_{\mathsf{letrec}})}}
as for
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
(see
definition

4.13
).
On the signature
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
we define the CRS
\textit{{Reg}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
(the CRS
\textit{{Reg}}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
) with the rules as the union of the rules of
\textit{{R}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\boldsymbol{\triangledown}}
and
Reg
(the union of the rules of
\textit{{R}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\boldsymbol{\triangledown}}
and
\textit{{Reg}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
).

§ 5.3

(list of rules for
\textit{{Reg}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\textit{{Reg}}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
)
.

The rules of
\textit{{Reg}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\textit{{Reg}}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
consist of the set of rules arising from the rule schemes

\varrho_{\triangledown}^{λ}
,
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{@}
,
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\mathrm{letrec}}
,
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\mathrm{rec}}
,
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\mathrm{nil}}
,
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\mathrm{red}}

from the unfolding CRS
\textit{{R}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\boldsymbol{\triangledown}}
, joined with the rules arising from the rule schemes

\varrho_{\Delta}^{@_{0}}
,
\varrho_{\Delta}^{@_{1}}
,
\varrho_{\Delta}^{λ}
,
\varrho_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{del}}
/
\varrho_{\Delta}^{\mathsf{S}}

from the decomposition CRS
Reg
/
\textit{{Reg}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
.

Notation 5.4

(rewrite relations for
\textit{{Reg}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\textit{{Reg}}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
)
.

To avoid ambiguity when referring to the rewriting relations induced by the rules of
\textit{{Reg}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\textit{{Reg}}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, we will prefix the rule names of
\textit{{R}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\boldsymbol{\triangledown}}
with
\triangledown
. Thus, we will denote the induced rewrite relations for
\textit{{Reg}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\textit{{Reg}}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
as:

\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.λ}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.@}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{letrec}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{rec}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{nil}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{red}}
,

\mathrel{\to}_{@_{0}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{1}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{λ}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}
/
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}
.

Definition 5.5

(the ARSs
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
,
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
)
.

By
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, and
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
we denote the ARSs that result by restricting the ARSs induced by
\textit{{Reg}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, and
\textit{{Reg}}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, respectively, to the subset
{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}}\right)
of terms.

The lemmas below state a number of simple rewrite properties for
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
concerning the interplay between unfolding and decomposition steps.

Lemma 5.6
.

On
{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ_{\mathsf{letrec}})}}}}\right)
, the rewrite relations in
\textit{{Reg}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and
\textit{{Reg}}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
have the following properties:

(i)

\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}
one-step commutes with
\mathrel{\to}_{λ}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{0}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{1}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}
, and
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}
:

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}_{\triangledown}

\displaystyle⋅\mathrel{\to}_{λ}

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{λ}

\displaystyle⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}_{\triangledown}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}_{\triangledown}

\displaystyle⋅\mathrel{\to}_{@_{i}}

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{@_{i}}

\displaystyle⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}_{\triangledown}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ (i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}_{\triangledown}

\displaystyle⋅\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}

\displaystyle⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}_{\triangledown}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}_{\triangledown}

\displaystyle⋅\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}

\displaystyle⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}_{\triangledown}

(ii)

\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
have the same normal forms as
Reg
and
\textit{Reg}^{+}
, respectively:

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x}}}}\right)\;{x}
is the only term in
{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ_{\mathsf{letrec}})}}}}\right)
in
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg}}
-normal-form.
Every
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}
-normal-form in
{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ_{\mathsf{letrec}})}}}}\right)
is of the form

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)\;{x_{n}}
.

Proof.

The commutation properties in
item

i
are easy to verify by analysing the behaviour of the rewrite rules in
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and in
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
on the terms of
{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ_{\mathsf{letrec}})}}}}\right)
.

The statement in
item

ii
follows from
proposition

4.24

item

i
, and
item

viii
: Normal forms with respect to
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
can only be λ-terms without occurrences of
\mathsf{letrec}
, since every occurrence of
\mathsf{letrec}
in a
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term gives rise to a
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}
-redex.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

The rewrite relation
\mathrel{\to}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}
(to
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}
-normal-form in at most
\omega
steps) one-step commutes with
\mathrel{\to}_{λ}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{0}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{1}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}
, and
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}
:

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{λ}

\displaystyle\;\subseteq\;\mathrel{\to}_{λ}⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{@_{i}}

\displaystyle\;\subseteq\;\mathrel{\to}_{@_{i}}⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}\penalty\ \penalty\ \;(i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}

\displaystyle\;\subseteq\;\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}

\displaystyle\;\subseteq\;\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}

This implies, for prefixed terms that have unfoldings that:

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{λ{y}.\,{L_{0}}}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}\mathrel{\to}_{λ}\llbracket\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}y}}}}\right)\;{L_{0}}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{{L_{0}}\;{L_{1}}}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}\mathrel{\to}_{@_{i}}\llbracket\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{L_{i}}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \;(i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\})

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{M}\penalty\ ⇒\penalty\ \llbracket\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}\llbracket\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{M}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{M}\penalty\ ⇒\penalty\ \llbracket\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}\llbracket\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{M}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}

Furthermore it holds:
\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}\penalty\ \subseteq\penalty\ \mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}
.

Proof.

The commutation properties with the rewrite relation
\mathrel{\to}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}
can be shown by using refined versions of the commutation properties in
lemma

5.6

item

i
, in which the minimal depth of unfolding steps is taken account of. When denoting by
\mathrel{\to}^{≥n}_{\triangledown}
the rewrite relation that is generated by
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}
-steps of depth
≥n
, then the following properties hold:

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{≥n}_{\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{λ}

\displaystyle\;\subseteq\;\mathrel{\to}_{λ}⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{≥n}_{\triangledown}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{≥n+1}_{\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{@_{i}}

\displaystyle\;\subseteq\;\mathrel{\to}_{@_{i}}⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{≥n}_{\triangledown}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{≥n+1}_{\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}

\displaystyle\;\subseteq\;\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{≥n}_{\triangledown}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{≥n+1}_{\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}

\displaystyle\;\subseteq\;\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}⋅\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{≥n}_{\triangledown}

Using these properties, strongly convergent
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}
-rewrite-sequences can be shown to project, via
\mathrel{\to}_{λ}
-,
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{i}}
-,
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}
-, and
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}
-steps, to strongly convergent
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}
-rewrite-sequences.

The property
\mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}\penalty\ \subseteq\penalty\ \mathrel{\leftarrow}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}
can be shown by using refined versions of the elementary diagrams from the confluence proof that take the minimal depths of steps into account.
∎

§ 5.8

(scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
)
.

As for
Reg
/
\textit{Reg}^{+}
we require of scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategies to have deterministic
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}
/
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}
-steps. As we did for
definition

4.32
, we will also fix all non-determinism except for the choice between
@_{0}
and
@_{1}
.

Definition 5.9

(scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
)
.

A strategy
\mathbb{S}
for
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
(
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
) will be called a
scope-delimiting
(
scope
+
-delimiting
)
strategy
if:

∘

\mathbb{S}
is deterministic for sources of
\mathrel{\to}_{λ}
-steps,
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}
-steps (
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}
-steps), and all
\mathsf{letrec}
-unfolding steps (i.e. all
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\rho}
-steps for every rule
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\rho}
of
\textit{{R}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\boldsymbol{\triangledown}}
).

∘

\mathbb{S}
enforces eager application of
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\mathrm{red}}
: every source of a step in
\mathbb{S}
according to an application of a rule different from
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\mathrm{red}}
is not the source of a
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{red}}
-step in the underlying ARS.

We say that such a strategy
\mathbb{S}
is a
lazy-unfolding
scope-delimiting strategy (a
lazy-unfolding
scope
+
-delimiting strategy) if furthermore:

∘

\mathbb{S}
applies the rules of
\textit{{R}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\boldsymbol{\triangledown}}
except for
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\mathrm{red}}
only at the root of the term, i.e. directly beneath the abstraction prefix.

∘

\mathbb{S}
uses the rules of
\textit{{R}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\boldsymbol{\triangledown}}
other than
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\mathrm{red}}
in a lazy way: every source of a step in
\mathbb{S}
with respect to a rule of
\textit{{R}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\boldsymbol{\triangledown}}
other than
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\mathrm{red}}
is not also the source of a step in the underlying ARS, with respect to one of the rules of
Reg
(of
\textit{Reg}^{+})
.

Notation 5.10
.

For every scope-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}
on
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
(on
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
), we denote by
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}.\rho}
the rewrite relation that is induced by those steps according to
\mathbb{S}
due to applications of the rule
\rho
of
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
(of
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
).

§ 5.11

(deterministic unfolding)
.

Note that in
definition

5.9
we do not only require of a strategy to eliminate the non-determinism with respect to
\varrho_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{del}}
-steps (
\varrho_{\Delta}^{\mathsf{S}}
-steps) but all non-determinism except for the
\varrho_{\Delta}^{@_{0}}
/
\varrho_{\Delta}^{@_{1}}
-non-determinism. This restriction will play a role later for the definition of λ-transition-graphs in
section

9
, and aso in this section for defining projections of scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategies for
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms to scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategies for λ-terms.

§ 5.12

(eager application of
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\mathrm{red}}
)
.

By requiring scope/scope
+
delimiting strategies to apply
\varrho_{\triangledown}^{\mathrm{red}}
eagerly we can exploit a useful property with respect to free variables of a term: if
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{L∈{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ_{\mathsf{letrec}})}}}}\right)}
is in
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{red}}
-normal-form, then the free variables occurring in
M
correspond to the free variables of
\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}
.

§ 5.13

(unfolding of prefixed terms)
.

Note that we have just applied
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}
to a prefixed term while in
definition

7.1

\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}
is only defined for ‘normal’, unprefixed terms from
{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}}\right)
. We will continue to do so in the rest of this section and assume that the domain of
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}
is extended in the obvious way to terms in
{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ_{\mathsf{letrec}})}}}}\right)
.

Definition 5.14

(
\mathbb{S}
-productive terms)
.

Let
L
be a
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term, and
\mathbb{S}
a scope-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
or a scope
+
-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
. We say that
L
is
\mathbb{S}
-productive
if every infinite rewrite sequence on
L
with respect to
\mathbb{S}
contains infinitely many steps according to
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.@_{0}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.@_{1}}
, or
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.λ}
.

Definition 5.15

(generated subterms of
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms)
.

We extend the domain of
{\mathrm{ST}}
from
definition

4.39
to
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms.
Let
\mathbb{S}
be a scope-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
. For every
L∈{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}}\right)
,
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L}}\right)
is defined as the set of objects of the sub-ARS
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}}}}\right)
, or in other words, the set of
\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}}
-reducts of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}}\penalty\ :\penalty\ {\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \textrightarrow\penalty\ \powerset({\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ_{\mathsf{letrec}})}}}}\right))

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\penalty\ ↦\penalty\ O\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \text{where $\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{O,\Phi,{\mathrm{src}},{\mathrm{tgt}}}}}\right\rangle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}}}}\right)$ }

§ 5.16
.

The following lemma states that every scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
(on
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms), when restricted to the reducts of a
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term
L
that expresses a λ-term
M
, projects to the restriction of a scope
+
-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg}^{+}
(on λ-terms) to reducts of
M
. And it asserts a similar statement for scope-delimiting strategies. The proof uses the commutation properties described in
lemma

5.7
between the infinite unfolding
\mathrel{\to}^{!\,\omega}_{\triangledown}
and the decomposition rewrite relations
\mathrel{\to}_{λ}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{0}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{1}}
,
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}
, and
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}
.

Lemma 5.17

(projection of scope-delimiting strategies)
.

Let
\mathbb{S}
be a scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}
for
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, and let
L
be a
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term that is
\mathbb{S}
-productive. Then there exists a (history-aware) scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\check{\mathbb{S}}
for
Reg
/
\textit{Reg}^{+}
such that the generated sub-ARS
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\check{\mathbb{S}}}}}\right)
of
\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}
is the projection (under the unfolding mapping
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}
) of the generated sub-ARS
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}}}}\right)
of
L
, in the sense that for all
L^{\prime}
in
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}}}}\right)
it holds:

\displaystyle L^{\prime}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}.\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}.\Delta}L^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ \Longrightarrow\penalty\ \llbracket L^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}\mathrel{\to}_{\check{\mathbb{S}}}\llbracket L^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}

\displaystyle\llbracket L^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}\mathrel{\to}_{\check{\mathbb{S}}}M^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ \Longrightarrow\penalty\ (∃L^{\prime\prime})\penalty\ L^{\prime}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}.\triangledown}⋅\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}.\Delta}L^{\prime\prime}\;∧\;M^{\prime\prime}=\llbracket L^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}

As a consequence,
\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}
is
\check{\mathbb{S}}
-regular if
L
is
\mathbb{S}
-regular.

Proof sketch.

We can utilise
lemma

5.7
to make commuting diagrams out of the two formulas above (for any given
L^{\prime}
), which allows us to determine
\check{\mathbb{S}}
with respect to all terms in
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}}}}\right)
. This freedom in the definition of
\check{\mathbb{S}}
also guarantees the property in the second implication in the lemma.
∎

Definition 5.18

(
\textit{{Parse}}_{\triangledown}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
)
.

By
\textit{{Parse}}_{\triangledown}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
we denote the CRS comprising the rules of
\textit{{Parse}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
as well as the unfolding rules from
\textit{{R}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\boldsymbol{\triangledown}}
.

Example 5.19
.

When applied to
\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{f}
, the rewrite relation in
\textit{{Parse}}_{\triangledown}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
unfolds and decomposes, but at the same time recreates the corresponding λ-term (see also
example

2.4
and
example

4.55
). In the rewriting sequence below, we prefix the rewrite relations that are due to rules from
\textit{{R}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\boldsymbol{\triangledown}}
with
\triangledown
, while the rewrite relations due to rules from
\textit{{Parse}}_{\triangledown}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
are not prefixed.

\displaystyle\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{f}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{rec}}{}

\displaystyle\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.λ}{}

\displaystyle\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{λ{x}.\,{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{λ{y}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{λ}{}

\displaystyle λ{x}.\,{\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{λ{y}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.λ}{}

\displaystyle λ{x}.\,{\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x}}}}\right)\;{λ{y}.\,{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{λ}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}.\,{\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.@}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}{.\,{\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{{f}\;{y}})}}\right)\\
\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{x})}}\right)\end{array}}}}\right)}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{red}}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}.\,{\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{{(\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{{f}\;{y}})}\;{(\mathsf{let}\;{}\;\mathsf{in}\;{x})}}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{nil}}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}.\,{\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{{(\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{{f}\;{y}})}\;{x}}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{@}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}{.\,{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{{f}\;{y}}}}}\right))}}\right)\\
\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{x}}}\right))}}\right)\end{array}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}{.\,{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{{f}\;{y}}}}}\right))}}\right)\\
\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x}}}}\right)\;{x}}}\right))}}\right)\end{array}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{0}}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}.\,{{(\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{{f}\;{y}}}}}\right))}\;{x}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.@}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}{.\,{{(\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{f})}}\right)\\
\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{y})}}\right)\end{array}}}}\right))}\;{x}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{red}}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}.\,{{(\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{{(\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{f})}\;{(\mathsf{let}\;{}\;\mathsf{in}\;{y})}}}}\right))}\;{x}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{nil}}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}.\,{{(\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{{(\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{f})}\;{y}}}}\right))}\;{x}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{@}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}{.\,{{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{f}}}}\right))}}\right)\\
\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{y}}}\right))}}\right)\end{array}}\;{x}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{0}}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}.\,{{{(\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,y,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{xy}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{f}}}}\right))}\;{y}}\;{x}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}.\,{{{(\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{f}}}}\right))}\;{y}}\;{x}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}.\,{{{(\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=λ{xy}.\,{{{f}\;{y}}\;{x}}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{f}}}}\right))}\;{y}}\;{x}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{rec}}{}

\displaystyle λ{xy}.\,{{{\penalty\ \dots\penalty\ }\;{y}}\;{x}}

Lemma 5.20
.

For all closed
L∈{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}}\right)
the following statements are equivalent:

(i)

L
expresses an infinite λ-term
M
, that is,
L\mathrel{\to}^{\omega}_{\triangledown}M
.

(ii)

\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}}}\right)\mathrel{\to}^{\omega}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}_{\triangledown}}}M
, for some infinite λ-term
M
.

(iii)

L
is
\mathbb{S}^{+}
-productive for some scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}^{+}
.

(iv)

L
is
\mathbb{S}^{+}
-productive for every scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}^{+}
.

Proof.

Let
L∈{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}}\right)
. We show the lemma by establishing the implications in the following order:
“
item

iv
⇒
item

iii
⇒
item

ii
⇒
item

i
⇒
item

iv
”.

The implication “
item

iv
⇒
item

iii
” is clear:
item

iv
implies that
L
is productive for e.g. the lazy-unfolding, eager scope
+
-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
.

For showing the implication “
item

iii
⇒
item

ii
”, let
\mathbb{S}
be a scope
+
-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg}^{+}
such that
L
is
\mathbb{S}
-productive. Then the strategy
\mathbb{S}
defines a
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}_{\triangledown}}}
-rewrite-sequence
\tau
on
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}}}\right)
by using
\mathbb{S}
to define next steps on subexpressions that are of the form
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\dots,\mathsf{pre}_{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}}]\hskip 1.0pt{P}}}\right)}}\right)
in already obtained reducts: if on a term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}}}}}\right)\;{P}
the strategy
\mathbb{S}
prescribes a
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}
-step, then this step is adopted in
\tau
; if
\mathbb{S}
prescribes a
\mathrel{\to}_{λ}
-step, then
\tau
can continue with a
{\mathrel{\to}}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}}.λ}
-step; if
\mathbb{S}
prescribes a
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{0}}
- and a
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{1}}
-step, then
\tau
can continue with a
{\mathrel{\to}}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}}.@}
-step. For the construction of
\tau
, possible steps in subexpressions
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\dots,\mathsf{pre}_{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}}]\hskip 1.0pt{P}}}\right)}}\right)
at parallel positions have to be interleaved to ensure that the reduction work is done in an outermost-fair way. Productivity of
\mathbb{S}
on
L
ensures that always after finitely many steps inside a subexpression
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\dots,\mathsf{pre}_{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}}]\hskip 1.0pt{P}}}\right)}}\right)
the function symbol
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{n}
disappears at this position (either entirely, or it is moved deeper over a λ-abstraction or an application). In the terms of the rewrite sequence
\tau
larger and larger λ-term contexts appear at the head. Hence
\tau
is strongly convergent, and it obtains, in the limit, an infinite λ-term; thus it witnesses
\tau:\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}}}\right)\mathrel{\to}^{\omega}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}_{\triangledown}}}M
.

For the implication “
item

ii
⇒
item

i
”, suppose that
\tau
is a rewrite sequence that witnesses
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}}}\right)\mathrel{\to}^{\omega}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}_{\triangledown}}}M
for some infinite λ-term
M
. Since the
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}}}
-steps require already unfolded parts of the term, they have to ‘shadow’ unfolding steps. All
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}
-steps in
\tau
take place beneath symbols
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{n}
. So the possibility of
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}}}
-steps during
\tau
depends on the unfolding steps during
\tau
, but not vice versa. Hence a rewrite sequence
\tau^{\prime}
on
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}}}\right)
can be constructed that only adopts the
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}
-steps from
\tau
. Since
\tau
is strongly convergent and converges to
M
,
\tau^{\prime}
witnesses
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}}}\right)\mathrel{\to}^{\omega}_{\triangledown}\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}}}\right)
. By dropping the ‘non-participant’ prefix context
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\Box}}}\right)
from all terms in
\tau^{\prime}
, and adapting the steps accordingly, a rewrite sequence
\tau^{\prime\prime}
is obtained that witnesses
\tau^{\prime\prime}:L\mathrel{\to}^{\omega}_{\triangledown}M
.

We show the implication “
item

i
⇒
item

iv
” indirectly. So we assume that there is a scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}
such that
L
is not
\mathbb{S}
-productive. As in the proof above of “
item

iii
⇒
item

ii
”,
\mathbb{S}
defines an outermost-fair
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}_{\triangledown}}}
-rewrite-sequence
\tau
on
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}}}\right)
. But since
\mathbb{S}
here is a strategy that is not productive for
L
, it follows that, due to its construction,
\tau
does not succeed in ‘pushing’ all function symbols
\mathsf{letrec}
to deeper and deeper depth, and thereby building up an infinite λ-term. Instead, this outermost-fair
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}_{\triangledown}}}
-rewrite-sequence contains infinitely many steps at the position of an outermost occurrence of
\mathsf{letrec}
. Since, other than the
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}
-steps, the
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}}}
-steps (which always take place above outermost occurrences of
\mathsf{letrec}
-symbols) cannot be the reason for this, the same stagnation of an outermost-fair unfolding process takes place if the
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{parse^{+}}}
-steps are postponed, that is dropped from
\tau
. In this way, by again dropping the ‘non-participant’ prefix context
\mathsf{parse}^{\mathrm{+}}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\Box}}}\right)
from the terms of
\tau
, and adapting the steps accordingly, we obtain an outermost-fair
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown}
-rewrite-sequence starting on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
that does not converge to an infinite λ-term. But then
lemma

7.13
implies that
L
does not unfold to an infinite λ-term.
∎

6
Proving regularity and strong regularity

§ 6.1

(overview)
.

In this section we introduce proof systems that are sound and complete for the notions of regular, and strongly regular λ-terms. In order to prove soundness and completeness, we establish, as auxiliary results, a correspondence between scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategies for
Reg
/
\textit{Reg}^{+}
and closed derivations in the corresponding proof systems. Then we introduce a proof system that is sound and complete for equality between strongly regular λ-terms. Furthermore, we give two proof systems that are sound and complete for the property of
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms to unfold to λ-terms. And finally, we show the following part of our characterisation result: λ-terms that are unfoldings of
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms are strongly regular.

We start with a more formal definition of λ-terms and
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms than
definition

4.21
, by means of derivability in a proof system that formalises term decomposition.

\mathsf{0}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}y}}}}\right)\;{y}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}y}}}}\right)\;{M_{0}}

λ

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{λ{y}.\,{M_{0}}}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M_{0}}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M_{1}}

@

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{{M_{0}}\;{M_{1}}}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n-1}}}}}\right)\;{M}

\mathsf{S}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\small(if the binding $λx_{n}$ is vacuous)}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)\;{M}

Proof system

for defining the set of λ-terms.

The set of plain λ-terms are those terms that comply with the previous definition when equipped with an empty prefix:

Definition 6.4

(proof systems
\mathbf{Reg}
, and
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
,
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
)
.

The natural-deduction style proof system
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
for recognising strongly regular λ-terms contains the axioms and rules as shown in
fig.

15
. In particular, the rule
\mathrm{FIX}
is a natural-deduction style derivation rule in which marked assumptions from the top of the proof tree can be discharged. Instances of this rule carry the side-condition that the depth 0pt
D
_0 of the immediate subderivation
\mathcal{D}_{0}
of its premise is greater or equal to 1 (hence this subderivation contains at least one rule instance, and, importantly, for a topmost occurrence of
\mathrm{FIX}
,
\mathcal{D}_{0}
must have a bottommost instance of one of the rules
(λ)
,
(@)
, or
(\mathsf{S})
).

The variant
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
of
\mathbf{Reg}
contains the same axioms and rules as
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, but in it instances of
\mathrm{FIX}
are subject to the additional side-condition: for all
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
on threads in
\mathcal{D}_{0}
from open marked assumptions
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M})^{u}
downwards it holds that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{y}}}}\right|≥\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}}}}\right|
.

The natural-deduction style proof system
\mathbf{Reg}
for recognising regular λ-terms differs from
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
by the absence of the rule
(\mathsf{S})
, and the presence instead of the rule
(\mathrm{del})
in
fig.

16
, and by the restriction of the axiom scheme (
\mathsf{0}
) to the more restricted version displayed in
fig.

16
.

Provability of a term in
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
in one of these proof systems is defined as the existence of a
closed
derivation: for
\boldsymbol{R}∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathbf{Reg},\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}},\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}}}}\right\}
we denote by
\vdash_{\boldsymbol{R}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}}
the existence of a proof tree
D
with conclusion
M
and with rule instances of
\boldsymbol{R}
such that all marked assumptions at the top of the
D
are discharged at some instance of the rule
\mathrm{FIX}
.

Remark 6.5

(
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
versus
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
)
.

While it will be established in
proposition

6.14
that provability in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
and
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
coincides, the difference between these systems will come to the fore in annotated versions that are purpose-built for the extraction of
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms that express λ-terms. This will be explained and illustrated later in
example

8.6
, using annotated versions of the two derivations in
example

6.8
above.

Remark 6.6
.

The proof system
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
is related to a proof system for nameless, finite terms in the λ-calculus that is used in
[
47
, sec. 2]
as part of a translation of λ-terms into ‘Lambdascope’ interaction nets, which are used for optimal evaluation (in the sense of Lévy) of λ-terms.

The proposition below explains that the side-condition on instances of
\mathrm{FIX}
from the proof systems above to have immediate subderivations
\mathcal{D}_{0}
with
0pt{\mathcal{D}_{0}}≥1
entails a ‘guardedness’ property for threads from such instances upwards to discharged instances.

Proposition 6.7

(cycles are guarded)
.

Let
D
be a derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}
, in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
or in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
possibly with open marked assumptions. Then for all instances
\iota
of the rule
\mathrm{FIX}
in
D
it holds: every thread from
\iota
upwards to a marked assumption that is discharged at
\iota
passes at least one instance of a rule
(λ)
or
(@)
.

Proof.

Since for
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
and
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
the argument is analogous, we only consider derivations in
\mathbf{Reg}
. So, let
D
be a derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}
. Furthermore, let
\iota
be an instance of the rule
\mathrm{FIX}
in
D
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}
, and let
\pi
be a thread from the conclusion of
\iota
upwards to a marked assumption
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M})^{u}
. Let
\kappa
be the topmost instance of
\mathrm{FIX}
in
D
that is passed on
\pi
. By its side-condition, the immediate subderivation of
\kappa
has depth greater or equal to 1, and hence there is at least one instance of a rule
(λ)
,
(@)
, or
(\mathrm{del})
passed on
\pi
above
\kappa
. If there is an instance of
(λ)
or
(@)
on this part of
\pi
, we are done. Otherwise only rules
(\mathrm{del})
are passed on
\pi
above
\kappa
. But since the rule
(\mathrm{del})
decreases the length of the abstraction prefix in the term occurrences in a pass from the conclusion to the premise, and since the length of the abstraction prefix at the start of
\pi
is the same as at the end of
\pi
, namely
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}}}}\right|
, it follows that at least one occurrence of a rule that increases the length of the abstraction prefix must also have been passed on
\pi
, on the segment from
\iota
to
\kappa
. Since the only rule of
\mathbf{Reg}
that increases the length of an abstraction prefix in a pass from conclusion to a premise is the rule
(λ)
, we have also in this case found a desired rule instance on
\pi
.
∎

Example 6.8

(difference between
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
and
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
)
.

Let
M

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}

Note that the first derivation is also a derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, but that this is not the case for the secord derivation, as for the occurrence of
\mathrm{FIX}
the side-condition in the system
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
is violated: on the path from the marked assumption
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x}}}}\right)\;{λ{y}.\,{{{M}\;{y}}\;{x}}})^{u}
down to the instance of
\mathrm{FIX}
there is the occurrence
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
of a term with shorter prefix than the term in the assumption and in the conclusion.

See
example

4.47
for a rewriting sequence in
\textit{Reg}^{+}
corresponding to the leftmost path in both derivations, and also
fig.

12
for the corresponding transition graph.

Example 6.9

(difference between
Reg
and
\textit{{Reg}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
)
.

The infinite λ-term from
example

2.2
with the
Reg
/
\textit{{Reg}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-transition-graphs shown in

is derivable in
\mathbf{Reg}
by the following closed derivation using the notation from

:

(\overbrace{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{b}}}}\right)\;{{rec_{M}}(b)}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{a}}}}\right)\;{{rec_{M}}(a)}})^{u}

\mathrm{del}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{ab}}}}\right)\;{{rec_{M}}(b)}

\mathsf{0}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{a}}}}\right)\;{a}

\mathrm{del}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{ab}}}}\right)\;{a}

@

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{ab}}}}\right)\;{{{rec_{M}}(b)}\;{a}}

λ

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{a}}}}\right)\;{λ{b}.\,{{{rec_{M}}(b)}\;{a}}}

\mathrm{FIX},u

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{a}}}}\right)\;{{rec_{M}}(a)}

λ

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{λ{a}.\,{{rec_{M}}(a)}}

When trying to construct a derivation for this term in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
from the bottom upwards, the rules of
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
apart from

offer only deterministic choices, resulting in an infinite proof tree of the form:

\vdots

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{abcde}}}}\right)\;{{rec_{M}}(e)}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{abcd}}}}\right)\;{λ{e}.\,{{rec_{M}}(e)}}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{abc}}}}\right)\;{c}

\mathsf{0}

But then, since this proof tree does not contain repetitions, the use of the rule

.

For the
Reg
and

. The corresponding transition graphs are displayed in

.

Proposition 6.10

(correspondence between proof systems and decomposition CRSs)
.

Let
D
be a derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}
/
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}
.
Then every path in
D
from the conclusion upwards corresponds to a
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg}}
/
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}
-rewrite-sequence from
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}
: passes over instances of
\mathrm{FIX}
correspond to empty rewrite steps; passes over instances of
(@)
to the left and to the right correspond to
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{0}}
- and
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{1}}
-steps, respectively; passes over instances of
(λ)
correspond to
\mathrel{\to}_{λ}
-steps; passes over instances of
(\mathrm{del})
/
(\mathsf{S})
correspond to
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}
/
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}
-steps.

The same holds for (finite or infinite) cyclic paths in
D
that return, possibly repeatedly, from a marked assumption at the top down to the conclusion of the instance of
\mathrm{FIX}
at which the respective assumption is discharged.

Proof.

The proposition is an easy consequence of the following facts: passes from a term in the conclusion of an instance
\iota
of one of the rules
(λ)
,
(\mathrm{del})
,
(\mathsf{S})
to the term in the premise of
\iota
correspond to
\mathrel{\to}_{λ}
-,
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}
-, and
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}
-steps, respectively; passes from a term in the conclusion of an instance of
(@)
to the left and the right premise correspond to
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{0}}
-steps and
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{1}}
-steps, respectively.
∎

§ 6.11

(proofs and scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategies)
.

Observe that, for the derivation
D
in
example

6.8
, the
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}
-rewrite-sequences that correspond to paths in
D
as described in
proposition

6.10
are actually rewrite sequences with respect to the
eager
scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}
for
\textit{Reg}^{+}
. This illustrates the general situation, formulated by the lemma below: paths in a derivation
D
in
\mathbf{Reg}
/
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
from the conclusion upwards correspond to rewrite sequences according to some – usually history-aware – scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}
, which can be extracted from
D
.

Lemma 6.12

(from
\mathbf{Reg}
/
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-derivations to scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategies)
.

Let
M∈{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}}}\right)
, and let
D
be a closed derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}
(in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
) with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
. Then there exists an, in general history-aware, scope-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
for
Reg
(scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
for
\textit{Reg}^{+}
) with the following properties:

(i)

Every (possibly cyclic) path in
D
from the conclusion upwards corresponds to a rewrite sequence with respect to
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
starting on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
in the sense of
proposition

6.10
where passes over instances of the rules
(@)
to the left and to the right correspond to
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}.@_{0}}
-steps and
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}.@_{1}}
-steps, respectively, and passes over instances of
(λ)
and of
(\mathrm{del})
(of
(\mathsf{S})
) correspond to
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}.λ}
-, and
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}.\mathrm{del}}
-steps (
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}.\mathsf{S}}
-steps).

(ii)

Every rewrite sequence that starts on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
and proceeds according to
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
corresponds to a (possibly cyclic) path in
D
starting at the conclusion in upwards direction: thereby a
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}.@_{0}}
-step and a
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}.@_{1}}
-step corresponds to a pass over (possibly successive
\mathrm{FIX}
-instances, or from a marked assumption to the instance of
\mathrm{FIX}
that binds it, followed by) an instance of
(@)
in direction left and right, respectively; a
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}.λ}
-step or
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}.\mathrm{del}}
-step (
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}.\mathsf{S}}
-step) corresponds to a pass over (possibly
\mathrm{FIX}
-instances and assumption bindings to
\mathrm{FIX}
-instances) an instance of
(λ)
or
(\mathrm{del})
(of
(\mathsf{S})
), respectively.

(iii)

{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {\text{the term $\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}$ occurs in \mathcal{D}}}}}\right\}
.

l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}y}}}}\right)\;{M_{0}}

λ

l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{λ{y}.\,{M_{0}}}

{l}\,0\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M_{0}}

{l}\,1\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M_{1}}

@

l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{{M_{0}}\;{M_{1}}}

\mathsf{0}

l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}y}}}}\right)\;{y}

l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n-1}}}}}\right)\;{M}

\mathsf{S}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\small\begin{tabular}[]{@{}l@{}}(if $x_{n}$ does not\\
occur in $M$)\end{tabular}}

l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)\;{M}

[l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}]^{u}

\mathcal{D}_{0}

l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}

\mathrm{FIX},u\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\small(if $0pt{\mathcal{D}_{0}}≥1$)}

l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}

Figure 17:
Proof system
\widehat{\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}}
for decorating
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-derivations with labels in
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}^{*}
.

Proof.

The proof defines a history-aware strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
for
\textit{Reg}^{+}
as a modification of an arbitrary (history-free) strategy for
\textit{Reg}^{+}
lifted to a labelled version of
\textit{Reg}^{+}
. Thereby the modification is performed according to a given derivation
D
, and the construction will guarantee that
item

i
,
item

ii
, and
item

iii
hold.

We establish the lemma only for the case of derivations in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, since the case of derivations in
\mathbf{Reg}
can be treated analogously. So, let
D
be a derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
.

In a first step we decorate
D
with position labels such that a derivation
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
with conclusion
\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\epsilon}}}}\right)\;{M}
in the variant proof system
\widehat{\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}}
in
fig.

17
is obtained. Note that the decoration process can be carried out in a bottom-up manner, where the label in the conclusion of a rule instance determines the label in the premise(s) if that is not an already labelled term, and where in the case of instances of the rule of
\mathrm{FIX}
also the labels in marked assumptions are determined.

In a second step we use the decorated version
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
of
D
to define a history-aware strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
according to which the term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
can be reduced as ‘prescribed’ by
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
. Since the derivations can only determine the strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
on terms occurring in
D
, we also have to define
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
on other terms. This will be done by choosing an arbitrary (but here: history-free) scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}
for
Reg
, and basing the definition of
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
on it.

We start by defining a labelling of
\textit{Reg}^{+}
as the ARS for which
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
will be defined as a history-free strategy, which together with an initial labelling
l
then yields a history-aware strategy for
\textit{Reg}^{+}
. Assuming
\textit{Reg}^{+}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right),\Phi,{\mathrm{src}},{\mathrm{tgt}}}}}\right\rangle
as the formal representation of
\textit{Reg}^{+}
, we define the ARS

\widehat{\textit{Reg}^{+}}:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{{\mathrm{\widehat{Ter}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right),\widehat{\Phi},\widehat{{\mathrm{src}}},\widehat{{\mathrm{tgt}}}}}}\right\rangle

where

\displaystyle{\mathrm{\widehat{Ter}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right):=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}∈{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right),{l}∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}^{*}}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N},\phi,l^{\prime}\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{N^{\prime}}}}}\right\rangle}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {\text{the following holds}}}}\right\}

there is an instance of
(λ)
,
(@)
, or
(\mathsf{S})
in
\widehat{\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}}
with
l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
in the conclusion and the term
l^{\prime}\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{N^{\prime}}
in the premise, and with
\phi:\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{N^{\prime}}
(one of) the corresponding step(s) in
\textit{Reg}^{+}

and where
\widehat{{\mathrm{src}}},\widehat{{\mathrm{tgt}}}:\widehat{\Phi}\textrightarrow{\mathrm{\widehat{Ter}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right)
are defined as projections on the first, and respectively, the third component of the triples that constitute steps in
\widehat{\Phi}
. Then the relation

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}:=

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{N},l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{N}}}}\right\rangle}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{N}∈{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right),{l}∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}^{*}}}}\right\}

\displaystyle{}∪\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{\phi,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{N},\phi,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{N}}}}\right\rangle}}}\right\rangle}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{N},\phi,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{N}}}}\right\rangle∈\widehat{\Phi}}}}\right\}

is a labelling of
\textit{Reg}^{+}
to
\widehat{\textit{Reg}^{+}}
.
As initial labelling we choose the function
l
that is defined by

l:{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right)\textrightarrow{\mathrm{\widehat{Ter}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right),\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}↦\epsilon\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}
.
and which adds the label ‘
\epsilon
’.

Now we define the strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
with:

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{{\mathrm{\widehat{Ter}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right),{\widehat{\Phi}}_{\text{on-}{{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}}}∪{\widehat{\Phi}}_{\text{not-on-}{{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}}},\widehat{{\mathrm{src}}}^{\prime},\widehat{{\mathrm{tgt}}}^{\prime}}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle{\widehat{\Phi}}_{\text{on-}{{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}}}:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N},\phi,l^{\prime}\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{N^{\prime}}}}}\right\rangle∈\widehat{\Phi}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {\text{the following holds}}}}\right\}

there is an instance of
(λ)
,
(@)
, or
(\mathsf{S})
in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
with
l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
in the conclusion and the term
l^{\prime}\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{N^{\prime}}
in the premise, and with
\phi:\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{N^{\prime}}
(one of) the corresponding step(s) in
\textit{Reg}^{+}

\displaystyle{\widehat{\Phi}}_{\text{not-on-}{{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}}}:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N},\phi,l^{\prime}\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{N^{\prime}}}}}\right\rangle∈\widehat{\Phi}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {\text{the following holds}}}}\right\}

l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
does not occur in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
,
\phi
is a step according to
\mathbb{S}

where
\widehat{{\mathrm{src}}}^{\prime}
,
\widehat{{\mathrm{tgt}}}^{\prime}
are the appropriate restrictions of
\widehat{{\mathrm{src}}}
and
\widehat{{\mathrm{tgt}}}
.

Note that, by its definition,
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
is a sub-ARS of
\widehat{\textit{Reg}^{+}}
. Now for showing that
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
is a (history-aware) strategy for
\widehat{\textit{Reg}^{+}}
, it has to be established that
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
is a history-free strategy for the lifted version
\widehat{\textit{Reg}^{+}}
of
\textit{Reg}^{+}
. For this it remains to show that every normal form of
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
is also a normal form of
\widehat{\textit{Reg}^{+}}
. So, let
l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}∈{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right)
be such that it is not a normal form of
\widehat{\textit{Reg}^{+}}
. Then also
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
is not a normal form of
\textit{Reg}^{+}
. For showing that there is a step in
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
with this labelled term as a source we will distinguish two cases, i.e. whether
l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
occurs on
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
or not.

For the second case, we assume that
l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
does not occur in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
. Then there is a step
\phi:(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{N^{\prime}})
in the scope-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}
in
\textit{Reg}^{+}
, which gives rise to the step
\phi:(l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})\mathrel{\to}(l^{\prime}\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}^{\prime}}}}}\right)\;{N^{\prime}})
in
\widehat{\textit{Reg}^{+}}
and in
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
.

For the first case, we assume that
l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
occurs in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
, and we fix an occurrence
o
. Since by assumption
l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
is not a normal form of
\widehat{\textit{Reg}^{+}}
,
o
cannot be the occurrence of an axiom (
\mathsf{0}
), and hence it is either an occurrence as the conclusion of an instance of one of the rules
(λ)
,
(@)
,
(\mathsf{S})
in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
, or as a marked assumption in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
. If
o
is the conclusion of an instance
\iota
of
(λ)
,
(@)
, or
(\mathsf{S})
, then
\iota
defines a step on
l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
which also is a step in
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
. If
o
is the conclusion of an instance of
\mathrm{FIX}
in
D
, then we consider an arbitrary path
\pi
in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
from
o
upwards towards a leaf of
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
. Since, due to the side-condition of the rule
\mathrm{FIX}
, immediate subderivations of instances of
\mathrm{FIX}
consist of at least one rule application,
\pi
cannot consist merely of applications of
\mathrm{FIX}
. Hence by following
\pi
from
o
upwards, after a number of successive instances of
\mathrm{FIX}
, each of which have
l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
as conclusion and premise, an instance of one of the rules
(λ)
,
(@)
,
(\mathsf{S})
follows, which witnesses a step with source
(λ)
,
(@)
,
(\mathsf{S})
in
\widehat{\textit{Reg}^{+}}
and in
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
. Finally, if
o
is an occurrence in a marked assumption at the top of the proof tree
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
, then, since
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
is a closed derivation and due to the form of instances of the assumption-discharging rule
\mathrm{FIX}
, there is also an occurrence
o^{\prime}
of
l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
as the conclusion of an instance of
\mathrm{FIX}
in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}
. Now the argument above can be applied to the occurrence
o^{\prime}
to obtain a step of
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
on
l\,:\,\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
.

By construction
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
conforms to
item

i
and
item

ii
because of the inclusion of
{\widehat{\Phi}}_{\text{on-}{{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}}}
and
{\widehat{\Phi}}_{\text{not-on-}{{\mathcal{D}}^{\smash{(\mathrm{lb})}}}}
respectively;
item

iii
follows from
item

ii
.
∎

Lemma 6.13

(from scope/scope
+
-delimiting strategies to
\mathbf{Reg}
/
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-derivations)
.

Let
M∈{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}}}\right)
, and let
\mathbb{S}
be a scope-delimiting strategy for
Reg
(a scope
+
-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg}^{+}
) such that
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
is finite. Then there exists a closed derivation
D
in
\mathbf{Reg}
(in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, and hence in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
) with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
such that the following properties hold (note the minor differences with the items
item

i
,
item

ii
, and
item

iii
in
lemma

6.12
):

(i)

Every (non-cyclic) path in
D
from the conclusion upwards to a leaf of the proof tree
D
corresponds to a
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}}
-rewrite-sequence starting on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
where passes over instances of the rules
(@)
to the left and to the right correspond to
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.@_{0}}
- and
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.@_{1}}
-steps, respectively, and passes over instances of
(λ)
and of
(\mathrm{del})
(of
(\mathsf{S})
) correspond to
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.λ}
-, and
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.\mathrm{del}}
-steps (
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.\mathsf{S}}
-steps); passes from the conclusion to the premise of instances of
\mathrm{FIX}
correspond to empty steps.

(ii)

Every sufficiently long
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}}
-rewrite-sequence on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}
has an initial segment that corresponds to a (non-cyclic) path in
D
from the conclusion upwards to a leaf of the proof tree: thereby a
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.@_{0}}
-step or
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.@_{1}}
-step corresponds to a pass over (possibly some
\mathrm{FIX}
-instances followed by) an instance of
(@)
in direction left and right, respectively; a
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.λ}
-step or
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.\mathrm{del}}
-step (
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.\mathsf{S}}
-step) corresponds to a pass over (possibly some
\mathrm{FIX}
-instances followed by) an instance of
(λ)
or
(\mathrm{del})
(of
(\mathsf{S})
), respectively.

(iii)

{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)\subseteq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {\text{the term }\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}\text{ occurs in }\mathcal{D}}}}\right\}
.

Proof.

We will argue only for the part of the statement of the lemma concerning a scope
+
-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg}^{+}
, since the case with a scope-delimiting strategy for
Reg
can be established analogously.

Let
M
be an λ-term, and let
\mathbb{S}
be a scope
+
-delimiting strategy for
Reg
such that
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
is finite. Now let
\mathcal{D}_{0}
be the (trivial) derivation with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
, which, in case that this is not an axiom of
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
(and
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
), is also an assumption, and then is of the form
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M})^{u}
, carrying an assumption marker
u
. If
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is an axiom, then it is easy to verify that the statements
item

i
,
item

ii
, and
item

iii
hold.

Otherwise we construct a sequence
\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2},\dots
of derivations where each
\mathcal{D}_{n}
satisfies the properties
item

i
,
item

ii
, and
item

iii
, where terms in marked assumptions are not also terms in axioms
\mathsf{0}
, and where
\mathcal{D}_{n+1}
extends
\mathcal{D}_{n}
by one additional rule instance above a marked assumption in
\mathcal{D}_{n}
: For the extension step on a derivation
\mathcal{D}_{n}
, a marked assumption
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})^{u}
in
\mathcal{D}_{n}
is picked with the property that the term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
does not appear in the thread down to the conclusion of
\mathcal{D}_{n}
.

Suppose that the
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}}
-rewrite-sequence from the conclusion of
\mathcal{D}_{n}
up to the marked assumption is of the form:

\tau:\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{0}}}}}\right)\;{M_{0}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{1}}}}}\right)\;{M_{1}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}}\dots\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{m}}}}}\right)\;{M_{m}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}

Note that, since by assumption
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
is not a term in an axiom
\mathsf{0}
of
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, it follows by
proposition

4.24

item

viii
, that it is not a
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}
-normal-form. Then depending on whether the possible next step(s) in an
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}}
-rewrite that extends
\tau
by one step is a
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.λ}
-,
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.\mathrm{del}}
-step, or either a
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.@_{0}}
-steps or a
\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.@_{1}}
-steps, the derivation
\mathcal{D}_{n}
is extended above the marked assumption
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})^{u}
by an application of λ,
\mathsf{S}
, or @, respectively. For example in the case that
\tau
extends by one additional step to either of the two rewrite sequences:

\displaystyle\tau_{i}:\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{0}}}}}\right)\;{M_{0}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}}\dots\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{m}}}}}\right)\;{M_{m}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{m}}}}}\right)\;{{M_{m,0}}\;{M_{m,1}}}\mathrel{\to}_{{\mathbb{S}}.@_{i}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{m}}}}}\right)\;{M_{m,i}}

with
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
, the derivation
\mathcal{D}_{n}
of the form:

\langle(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{m}}}}}\right)\;{{M_{m,0}}\;{M_{m,1}}})^{u}\rangle

\mathcal{D}_{n}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}

is extended to
\mathcal{D}_{n+1}
:

(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{m}}}}}\right)\;{M_{m,0}})^{u_{0}}

(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{m}}}}}\right)\;{M_{m,1}})^{u_{1}}

@

\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{m}}}}}\right)\;{{M_{m,0}}\;{M_{m,1}}}\rangle

\mathcal{D}_{n}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}

for two fresh assumption markers
u_{0}
and
u_{1}
(the angle brackets
\langle\dots\rangle
are used here to indicate just a single formula occurrence at the top of the proof tree
\mathcal{D}_{n}
). If either of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{m}}}}}\right)\;{M_{m,0}}
or
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{m}}}}}\right)\;{M_{m,1}}
is an axiom, then the assumption marker is removed and the formula is marked as an axiom
\mathsf{0}
, accordingly. Note that, if the statements
item

i
,
item

ii
, and
item

iii
are satisfied for
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{n}
, then this is also the case for
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{n+1}
. Furthermore, terms in marked assumptions are not terms in axioms of
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
.

The extension process continues as long as
\mathcal{D}_{n}
contains a marked assumption
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})^{v}
without a ‘
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-admissible repetition’ beneath it, by which we mean the occurrence
o
of the term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
on the thread down to the conclusion in
D
, but strictly beneath the marked assumption, such that furthermore all terms on the part of the thread down to
o
have an abstraction prefix of length greater or equal to
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{y}}}}\right|
. (Note the connection to the side-condition on instances of the rule
\mathrm{FIX}
in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, and, in particular, that marked assumptions with an
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-admissible repetition beneath it could be discharged by an appropriately introduced instance of
\mathrm{FIX}
in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
.)

That the extension process terminates can be seen as follows: Suppose that, to the contrary, it continues indefinitely. Then, since the derivation size increases strictly in every step, an infinite proof tree
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}
is obtained in the limit, which due to finite branchingness of the proof tree and Kőnig’s Lemma possesses an infinite path
\pi
starting at the conclusion. Now note that due to
item

i
,
\pi
corresponds to an infinite
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}}
-rewrite-sequence. Due to
proposition

4.50
, this infinite rewrite sequence must contain a grounded cycle. However, the existence of such grounded cycle contradicts the termination condition of the extension process, because every grounded cycle provides an
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-admissible repetition.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{N}
, for some
N∈ℕ
, be the derivation that is reached when no further extension step, as described, is possible. By the construction the statements
item

i
,
item

ii
, and
item

iii
are satisfied for
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{N}
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{D}_{N}
is a derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
and
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
in which every leaf at the top is either an axiom
\mathsf{0}
or an assumption
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})^{u}
marked with a unique marker
u
, and for every such marked assumption in
\mathcal{D}_{N}
, there is a
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-admissible repetition strictly beneath it. This fact enables us to modify
\mathcal{D}_{N}
into a closed derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
by closing all open assumptions by newly introduced applications of the rule
\mathrm{FIX}
. More precisely, steps of the following kind are carried out repeatedly. A derivation with occurrences of a number of marked assumptions
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})^{u_{i}}
highlighted together with a single occurrence of the term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
in its interior that indicates the
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-admissible repetition for the displayed marked assumptions:

\langle(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})^{u_{1}}\rangle

\dots

\langle(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})^{u_{k}}\rangle

\mathcal{D}_{000}

\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}\rangle

\mathcal{D}_{00}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}

is modified into:

\langle(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})^{w}\rangle

\dots

\langle(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})^{w}\rangle

\mathcal{D}_{000}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}

\mathrm{FIX},w

\langle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}\rangle

\mathcal{D}_{00}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}

where
w
is a fresh assumption marker. In every such transformation step the number of open assumptions is strictly decreased, but the properties
item

i
,
item

ii
, and
item

iii
(for
D
the resulting derivation of such a step) is preserved. Hence after finitely many such transformation steps a derivation
D
in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
without open assumptions and with the properties
item

i
,
item

ii
, and
item

iii
is reached, and obtained as the result of this construction.
∎

As a consequence of the two lemmas above, derivability in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
and in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
coincides:

Proposition 6.14

(
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
≡
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
)
.

∀M∈{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}}}\right)\penalty\ \penalty\ \vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}}\penalty\ \Longleftrightarrow\penalty\ \vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}}

Proof.

The direction “⇐” follows by the fact that every derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
is also a derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
. For the direction “⇒”, let
M
be an infinite term such that
\vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}}
. By
lemma

6.12
there exists a scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}^{+}
such that
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
is finite. But then it follows by
lemma

6.13
that there is also a closed derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
, and hence that
\vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}}
.
∎

Now we have assembled all auxiliary statements that we use for proving a theorem that tightly links derivability in the proof system
\mathbf{Reg}
with regularity, and derivability in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
and in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with strong regularity, of λ-terms.

Theorem 6.15
.

The following statements hold for the proof systems
\mathbf{Reg}
,
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
,
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
:

(i)

\mathbf{Reg}
is sound and complete for regular λ-terms. That is, for all
M∈{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}}}\right)
it holds:

\vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}}\hskip 21.52771pt\text{if and only if}\hskip 21.52771pt\text{$M$ is regular.}

(ii)

\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
and
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
are sound and complete for strongly regular λ-terms. That is,
for all
M∈{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}}}\right)
the following statements are equivalent:

(a)

M
is strongly regular.

(b)

\vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}}
.

(c)

\vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}}
.

Proof.

Since the proof of statement
item

ii
of the theorem can be carried out analogously (taking into account
proposition

6.14
), we argue here only for statement
item

i
.

For “⇒” in
item

i
, let
M
be a λ-term that is regular. Then there exists a scope-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}
on
Reg
such that
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
is finite. By
lemma

6.13
it follows that there exists a closed derivation
D
in
\mathbf{Reg}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
. This derivation witnesses
\vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}}
.
For “⇐” in
item

i
, suppose that
\vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}}
. Then there exists a closed derivation
D
in
\mathbf{Reg}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
. Now
lemma

6.12
entails the existence of a scope-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}
in
\textit{Reg}^{+}
such that, in particular,
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
is finite. This fact implies that
M
is regular.
∎
