For defining a proof system for equality of strongly regular λ-terms, we first give a specialised version of the proof system
\mathbf{A}^{\hskip-1.63885pt\boldsymbol{\infty}}_{\mathcal{K}}
(for α-equivalence of iCRS preterms) for
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
-preterms, and a corresponding system on
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
-terms.

Definition 6.16

(proof systems
\mathbf{EQ}_{\boldsymbol{α}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
,
{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
)
.

The proof system
\mathbf{EQ}_{\boldsymbol{α}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
for α-equivalence of infinite preterms in
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
consists of the rules displayed in
fig.

18
.
The proof system
{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
for equality of infinite terms in
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
consists of the rules displayed in
fig.

19
.
Provability in
\mathbf{EQ}_{\boldsymbol{α}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
and in
{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
is defined, analogous to the proof system
\mathbf{A}^{\hskip-1.63885pt\boldsymbol{\infty}}_{\mathcal{K}}
from
definition

3.24
, as the existence of a completed (possibly infinite) derivation, and will, as done for
\mathbf{A}^{\hskip-1.63885pt\boldsymbol{\infty}}_{\mathcal{S}}
in
definition

3.23
, be indicated using the symbol
\prescript{\infty}{}{\vdash}
.

Proposition 6.17
.

The following statements hold for the proof systems
\mathbf{EQ}_{\boldsymbol{α}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
and
{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
.

(i)

\mathbf{EQ}_{\boldsymbol{α}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
is sound and complete for
\equiv_{α}
on
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
-preterms. That is, the following holds for all closed preterms
s
and
t
in
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
:

\prescript{\infty}{}{\vdash}_{\mathbf{EQ}_{\boldsymbol{α}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}}\hskip 1.5pt{\mathsf{pre}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(s}}\right)=\mathsf{pre}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(t}}\right)}\hskip 17.22217pt\text{if and only if}\hskip 17.22217pts\equiv_{α}t.

(ii)

{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
is sound and complete for equality between
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
-terms. That is, the following holds for all terms
M
and
N
in
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
:

\prescript{\infty}{}{\vdash}_{{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}}\hskip 1.5pt{M=N}\hskip 17.22217pt\text{if and only if}\hskip 17.22217ptM=N.

Proof.

For statement
item

i
it suffices to show that, for an equation
\mathsf{pre}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(s}}\right)=\mathsf{pre}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(t}}\right)
between preterms of
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
, derivability in
\mathbf{EQ}_{\boldsymbol{α}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
coincides with derivability of this equation in the general proof system
\mathbf{A}^{\hskip-1.63885pt\boldsymbol{\infty}}_{\mathcal{K}}
for α-equivalence between iCRS preterms in
definition

3.24
. Given a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}
in
\mathbf{EQ}_{\boldsymbol{α}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
, a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{K}}
in
\mathbf{A}^{\hskip-1.63885pt\boldsymbol{\infty}}_{\mathcal{K}}
results by replacing each formula occurrence
\mathsf{pre}_{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{{x}_{1}\dots{x}_{n}}]\hskip 1.0pt{s}}}\right)=\mathsf{pre}_{n}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{{y}_{1}\dots{y}_{n}}]\hskip 1.0pt{t}}}\right)
by the formula occurrence
\{{{x}_{1}\dots{x}_{n}}\}\hskip 1.0pt{s}=\{{{y}_{1}\dots{y}_{n}}\}\hskip 1.0pt{t}
and adding an instance of the rule for the function symbol
\mathsf{pre}_{0}
at the bottom. Then instances of the axioms and rules (
\mathsf{0}
), (
@
),
(λ)
, and
(\mathsf{S})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}
correspond to instances of axioms and rules (
\mathsf{0}
), (
\mathsf{app}
), (
[{\hskip 1.0pt}]\hskip 1.0pt{}
), and
(\mathsf{S})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{K}}
, respectively. This proof transformation also has an inverse.

For “⇐” in statement
item

ii
it suffices to note: Every scope
+
-delimiting strategy for
\textit{Reg}^{+}
can be used to stepwise extend finite derivations in
{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
=
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
by one additional rule application above a leaf containing a formula
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{N}
=
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{N}
that is not an axiom
\mathsf{0}
, which implies that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{N}
is not a normal form of
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}
. If these extensions are carried out in a fair manner by extending all non-axiom leafs at depth
n
in the proof tree before proceeding with leafs at depth
>n
, then in the limit a completed derivation in
{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
is obtained.

For “⇒” in statement
item

ii
, suppose that
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}
is a completed derivation in
{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
=
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{N}
. Let
\mathsf{pre}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(s}}\right)
and
\mathsf{pre}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(t}}\right)
be preterm representatives of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{N}
, respectively. Now a completed derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}_{\text{pter}}
in
\mathbf{EQ}_{\boldsymbol{α}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
can be found by developing it step by step from the conclusion
\mathsf{pre}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(s}}\right)=\mathsf{pre}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(t}}\right)
upwards, parallel to
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}
, and following the rules of
\mathbf{EQ}_{\boldsymbol{α}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
, which are invertible (that is, the premises of a rule instance are uniquely determined by the conclusion). Then
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}_{\text{pter}}
is a preterm representative version of
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}
. By using
item

i
, it follows that
s\equiv_{α}t
. Since
s
and
t
are preterm representatives of
M
and
N
, respectively,
M=N
follows.
∎

Definition 6.18

(the proof system
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\boldsymbol{=}}

.

The natural-deduction-style proof system

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{N}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}
=
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{N}
.

\mathcal{D}_{0}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}

The rule
\mathrm{FIX}
, which is added to the rules of

Theorem 6.19
.

\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\boldsymbol{=}}
is sound and complete for equality between strongly regular λ-terms. That is, for all strongly regular λ-terms
M
and
N
it holds:

\vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\boldsymbol{=}}}{M=N}\hskip 17.22217pt\text{if and only if}\hskip 17.22217ptM=N.

Proof sketch.

Let
M
and
N
be strongly regular λ-terms. In view of
proposition

6.17

item

ii
, it suffices to show:

\vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\boldsymbol{=}}}{M=N}\hskip 17.22217pt\text{if and only if}\hskip 17.22217pt\prescript{\infty}{}{\vdash}_{{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}}\hskip 1.5pt{M=N}.

(1)

For showing “⇐” in
eq.

1
, let
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}
be a derivation in
{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
=
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{N}
. Since paths in
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}
correspond to
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}
-rewrite-sequences, and since the number of generated subterms of both
M
and
N
are finite (as a consequence of their strong regularity), on every infinite thread equation repetitions occur. These repetitions can be used to cut all infinite threads by appropriate introductions of instances of
\mathrm{FIX}
in order to obtain a finite and closed derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\boldsymbol{=}}
with the same conclusion.

For showing “⇒” in
eq.

1
, let
D
be a closed derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\boldsymbol{=}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
=
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{N}
. Now
D
can be unfolded into an infinite derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\infty}
in
{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
by repeatedly removing a bottommost instance of
\mathrm{FIX}
and inserting its immediate subderivation above each of the marked assumptions the instance discharges. If this process is organised in a fair manner with respect to bottommost instances of
\mathrm{FIX}
, then in the limit an infinite completed proof tree with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
=
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{N}
in
{\mathbf{EQ}}^{\boldsymbol{\infty}}
is obtained. For productivity of this process it is decisive that the side-condition on every instance
\iota
of the rule
\mathrm{FIX}
guarantees that on threads from the conclusion of
\iota
to a marked assumption that is discharged by
\iota
at least one instance of a rule different from
\mathrm{FIX}
is passed.
∎

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{f_{i}}}]^{u_{i}}}}}\right\}_{i=1,\dots,n}

\mathcal{D}_{j}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\dots\penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{{L_{j}}[{\vec{f}}:={\vec{\mathsf{c}}_{\vec{f}}}]}\penalty\ \dots}}}\right\}_{j=0,\dots,n}

\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}},u_{1},\dots,u_{n}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f_{1}=L_{1}\dots f_{n}=L_{n}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{L_{0}}}

where
\mathsf{c}_{f_{1}},\dots,\mathsf{c}_{f_{n}}
are distinct constants fresh for
L_{1},\dots,L_{n}
, and substitutions
{L_{j}}[{\vec{f}}:={\vec{\mathsf{c}}_{\vec{f}}}]
stands for
L_{j}[f_{1}:=\mathsf{c}_{f_{1}},\dots,f_{n}:=\mathsf{c}_{f_{n}}]
.

side-conditions
:
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{y}}}}\right|≥\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}}}}\right|
holds for the prefix length of every
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
on a thread in
\mathcal{D}_{j}
for

from an open assumptions
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{f_{i}}})^{u_{i}}
downwards; for bottommost instances: the arising derivation is guarded on access path cycles.

Figure 21:
The rule
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
for the natural-deduction style proof systems
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and

on
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms.

For the purpose of the following definition and the respective proof system in
fig.

21
we extend the signature
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
of
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
by an infinite set of constants for which we use the symbol
\mathsf{c}
as syntactical variables which frequently carry index subscripts.

Definition 6.20

(proof systems
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
,
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, and
\mathbf{ug-Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
,
\mathbf{ug-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
)
.

The proof systems
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
for
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms arise from the proof systems
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
and
\mathbf{Reg}
(see
definition

6.4
,
fig.

15
and
fig.

16
), respectively, by replacing the terms in the axioms (
\mathsf{0}
), and rules
(λ)
,
(@)
,
(\mathsf{S})
and
(\mathrm{del})
through
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms with abstraction prefixes accordingly, and by replacing the rule
\mathrm{FIX}
with the rule
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
in
fig.

21
. The side-condition concerning access path cycles on the derivation arising by an instance of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
pertains only to bottommost occurrences of this rule, and is explained below. By
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}^{-}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
we mean the variant of the rule
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
in which the side-condition concerning guardedness of the arising derivation on access path cycles has been dropped. By
\mathbf{ug-Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\mathbf{ug-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
we denote the variants of
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, respectively, in which the rule
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
is replaced by the rule
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}^{-}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
.

Let
D
be a derivation in one of these proof systems. By an
access path
of
D
we mean a (possibly cyclic) path
\pi
in
D
such that:

(a)

\pi
starts at the conclusion and can proceed in upwards direction;

(b)

at instances of
(@)
,
\pi
can step from the conclusion to one of the premises;

(c)

at instances of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
,
\pi
can step from the conclusion to the rightmost premise (which corresponds to the body of the
\mathsf{let}
-expression);

(d)

when arriving at a marked assumption
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{f_{i}}})^{u_{i}}
that is discharged at an application of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
of the form as displayed in
fig.

21
,
\pi
can step over to the conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{{L_{i}}[{\vec{f}}:={\vec{\mathsf{c}}_{\vec{f}}}]}
of the subderivation
\mathcal{D}_{i}
of that application of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
, and proceed from there, again in upwards direction.

For every formula occurrence
o
in
D
, by a
relative access path
from
o
we mean a path with the properties
item

b
–
item

d
that starts at
o
and proceeds in upwards direction. An access path (or relative access path) in
D
is
cyclic
if there is a formula occurrence in
D
that is visited more than once.

Now we say that
D
is
guarded on access path cycles
if every cyclic access path contains, on each of its cycles, at least one
guard
, that is, an instance of a rule
(λ)
or
(@)
. We say that
D
is
guarded
if every relative access path contains a guard on each of its cycles.

Example 6.21
.

The
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term
L_{1}=\mathsf{let}\;{f=f,\,g=λ{x}.\,{x}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{λ{y}.\,{g}}
admits the following closed derivation
\mathcal{D}_{1}
in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
:

(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{g}})^{u_{2}}

\mathrm{del}/\mathsf{S}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{g}}

λ

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{λ{y}.\,{\mathsf{c}_{g}}}

(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{f}})^{u_{1}}

\mathsf{0}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x}}}}\right)\;{x}

λ

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{λ{x}.\,{x}}

\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}},u_{1},u_{2}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=f,\,g=λ{x}.\,{x}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{λ{y}.\,{g}}}

This derivation can be built in a straightforward way, from the bottom upwards. Note that
D
is guarded on access path cycles, and hence that the instance of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
at the bottom is a valid one, because:
D
does not possess any cyclic access paths. In particular, there is no access path in
\mathcal{D}_{1}
that reaches the first premise of the instance of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
: this premise is the starting point of an unguarded relative access path, which entails that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
itself is not guarded.

Now consider the
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term
L_{2}=\mathsf{let}\;{f=f,\,g=λ{x}.\,{x}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{λ{y}.\,{{f}\;{g}}}
. When trying to construct a derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
for this term in a bottom-up manner, one arrives at the closed derivation
\mathcal{D}_{2}
in
\mathbf{ug-Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\mathbf{ug-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
:

(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{f}})^{u_{1}}

\mathrm{del}/\mathsf{S}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{f}}

(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{g}})^{u_{2}}

\mathrm{del}/\mathsf{S}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{g}}

@

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y}}}}\right)\;{{\mathsf{c}_{f}}\;{\mathsf{c}_{g}}}

λ

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{λ{y}.\,{{\mathsf{c}_{f}}\;{\mathsf{c}_{g}}}}

\hskip-30.1388pt(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{f}})^{u_{1}}

\mathsf{0}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x}}}}\right)\;{x}

λ

\hskip-4.30554pt\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{λ{x}.\,{x}}

\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}^{-}_{\mathsf{letrec}}},u_{1},u_{2}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{f=f,\,g=λ{x}.\,{x}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{λ{y}.\,{{f}\;{g}}}}

However,
\mathcal{D}_{2}
is not a valid derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, as the inference step at the bottom is
an instance of

, but not of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
,
because the side-condition on the arising derivation to be guarded on access path cycles is not satisfied:
now there is an access path that reaches the first premise of the derivation and that continues looping on
this an unguarded cycle.
Since the bottom-up search procedure for derivations is deterministic in this case,
it follows that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L_{2}}
is not derivable in
\mathbf{Reg}
nor in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
.

Similar as the correspondence, stated by
proposition

6.10
,
between (possibly cyclic) paths in a derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}
and

starting at the conclusion
and rewrite sequences with respect to

and

on the infinite term in the conclusion,
there is also the following correspondence between access paths in a derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
,
and rewrite sequences with respect to
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg}}
and
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}
on the
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term in the conclusion.

Proposition 6.22
.

Let
D
be a derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
or
\mathbf{ug-Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
(in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
or in
\mathbf{ug-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
) with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{L}
.

Then every access path in
D
to an occurrence
o
of a term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{P}
corresponds to a
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg}}
-rewrite-sequence
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{L}

\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathrm{reg}}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{B}\;\mathsf{in}\;{\tilde{P}}}
(to a
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}
-rewrite-sequence
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{L}

\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{B}\;\mathsf{in}\;{\tilde{P}}}
), where
B
arises as the union of all outermost binding groups in conclusions of instances of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
below
o
, and
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{P}
=
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{{\tilde{P}}[{\vec{f}}:={\vec{\mathsf{c}}_{\vec{f}}}]}
where
\vec{f}
is comprised of the function variables occurring in
B
and
\vec{\mathsf{c}}_{\vec{f}}
distinct constants for
\vec{f}
as chosen by
D
. More precisely:

(a)

a pass over an instance of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
corresponds to an empty or
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{letrec}}
-step, dependent on whether the instance is the bottommost
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
-instance or not;

(b)

a pass over an instance of the rule
(@)
to the left/to the right corresponds to a
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{0}}
-step/
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{1}}
-step, which, if the application is somewhere above an instance of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
, has to be preceded by a
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.@}
-step;

(c)

a pass over an instance of the rule
(λ)
corresponds to a
\mathrel{\to}_{λ}
-step which, if the application is above an instance of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
, has to be preceded by a
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.λ}
-step;

(d)

a pass over an instance of the rule
(\mathrm{del})
/
(\mathsf{S})
corresponds to a
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{del}}
/
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathsf{S}}
-step, possibly preceded by an application of
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{red}}
.

(e)

a step from a marked assumption to a premise of a
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
-instances, a step as described in
item

c
of the definition of access paths, corresponds to an
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{rec}}
-step followed by a
\mathrel{\to}_{\triangledown\hskip-0.81949pt.\mathrm{red}}
-step.

Lemma 6.24
.

Let
D
be a closed derivation in
\mathbf{ug-Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
(in
\mathbf{ug-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
) with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
. Then there exists a scope-delimiting (scope
+
-delimiting) strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
for
\textit{Reg\/}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
(for
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
) with the following properties:

(i)

Every access path in
D
corresponds to a rewrite sequence with respect to
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
starting on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
in the sense of
proposition

6.22
.

(ii)

Every rewrite sequence that starts on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
and proceeds according to
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
corresponds to an access path in
D
with correspondences as described in
proposition

6.22
.

(iii)

{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L}}\right)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{B}\;\mathsf{in}\;{\tilde{P}}}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {\begin{tabular}[]{@{}l@{}}$\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{let}\;{B}\;\mathsf{in}\;{\tilde{P}}}$ arises from an\\
occurrence of $\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{P}$ on an\\
access path of \mathcal{D}as described\\
in \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{prop:Regletrec:stRegletrec}\end{tabular}}}}\right\}
.

As a consequence of that
D
is finite,
L
is
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
-regular.

(iv)

L
is
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
-productive ⇔
D
is guarded (i.e.
D
derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
(
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
)).

Proof.

Given a closed derivation
D
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
(for example) in
\mathbf{ug-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, a scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
for
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
such that
item

i
–
item

iv
hold can be extracted from
D
similar as in the proof of a scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
in
\textit{Reg}^{+}
was extracted from a closed derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
. That the extracted strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
is productive (not productive) for
L
if
D
is guarded (not guarded) can be seen by the fact that
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
-rewrite-sequences correspond to access paths of
D
in the sense as stated by
proposition

6.22
.
∎

Now we will prove that derivability in
\mathbf{ug-Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\mathbf{ug-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
is guaranteed for all
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms, and that derivability in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
/
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
is a property of a
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term that is decidable by an easy parsing process.

Proposition 6.25
.

The following statements hold:

(i)

For every
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term
L
,
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
is derivable both in
\mathbf{ug-Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
and in
\mathbf{ug-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
.

(ii)

For every
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term
L
, derivability of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
is decidable in at most quadratic time in the size of
L
.

Proof.

For
item

i
note that for every
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term
L
, a closed derivation
\mathcal{D}_{L}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
in
\mathbf{ug-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
can be produced by a bottom-up construction following the term structure of
L
. Hereby use of the rules
(\mathsf{S})
can be restricted to instances immediately below marked assumptions such that, viewed from a (non-cyclic) path
\pi
from the conclusion upwards to a marked assumption, these
(\mathsf{S})
-instances are only introduced to shorten the frozen abstraction prefixes by all λ-abstractions that have become frozen on
\pi
(in order to conform to the side-condition on
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}^{-}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
-instances to have the same frozen abstraction prefix lengths in the discharged marked assumptions as in the conclusion and in the premises).

Now for
item

ii
in order to decide derivability of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, it suffices to decide whether the derivation
\mathcal{D}_{L}
in
\mathbf{ug-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
obtained as described above, or its bottommost instance of
\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}^{-}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}
if there is any, is guarded on all of its access path cycles. (Note that in the construction of
\mathcal{D}_{L}
only the freedom in placing instances of
(\mathsf{S})
has been used in a certain, namely lazy, way. The specific placement of instances of these rules does not interfere with the existence or non-existence of guards, that is instances of λ or @ on cycles of access paths.) For this it remains to check whether every cycle on an access path in
\mathcal{D}_{L}
has a guard. This can be done by exploring the proof tree of
\mathcal{D}_{L}
according to all possible access paths (until for the first time a cycle is concluded) and checking for the existence of guards on cycles.
∎

We now can prove soundness and completeness of the proof system
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
for the property of
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms to unfold to λ-terms.

Theorem 6.26
.

\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
is sound and complete for the property of
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms to unfold to a λ-term. That is, for every term
L∈{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}}\right)
the following statements are equivalent:

(i)

L
expresses a λ-term.

(ii)

\vdash_{\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}}
.

Proof.

For the proof of both directions of the equivalence, let
L∈{\mathrm{Ter}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}}\right)
.

For showing the implication
item

i
⇒
item

ii
, we argue indirectly, and therefore assume that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
is not derivable in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
. Then, while
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
is not derivable in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, there is, by
proposition

6.25

item

i
, a derivation
D
in
\mathbf{ug-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
that is not guarded. It follows by
lemma

6.24
, and in particular due to
lemma

6.24

item

iv
, that there is a scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
for
\textit{Reg}^{+}
such that
L
is not
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
-productive. Then it follows by
lemma

5.20
, using
item

i
⇒
item

iv
there, that
L
does not unfold to a λ-term.

For showing the implication
item

ii
⇒
item

i
, let
D
be a closed derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
. It follows by
lemma

6.24
that there is a scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}
for
Reg
such that
L
is
\mathbb{S}
-productive. Then
lemma

5.20
implies that
L
unfolds to a λ-term.
∎

§ 6.27

(soundness and completeness of
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
)
.

Also the proof system
\mathbf{Reg}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
can be shown to be sound and complete for the property of
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms to unfold to λ-terms. To establish this in analogy with the route of proof we pursued here, a CRS
Parse
similar to
\textit{{Parse}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
(see
definition

4.52
) could be defined by replacing the rule
\varrho_{\mathrm{parse^{+}}}^{\mathsf{S}}
by a rule
\varrho_{\mathrm{parse^{+}}}^{\mathrm{del}}
that can compress more abstraction prefixes, similar as the rule
\varrho_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\mathrm{del}}
of
Reg
can compress more abstraction prefixes than the rule
\varrho_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}^{\mathsf{S}}
of
\textit{{Reg}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
. Then furthermore also a lemma analogous to
lemma

5.20
can be formulated, proved, and used in a similar way.

We now arrive at a theorem that states one direction of our main characterisation result (
theorem

8.13
in
section

8
) that will link
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-expressibility to strong regularity of λ-terms.

Theorem 6.28
.

Every
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-expressible λ-term is strongly regular.

Proof.

Let
M
be a λ-term that is expressible by a
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term
L
, that is,
L\mathrel{\to}^{\omega}_{\triangledown}M
holds. Then by
theorem

6.26
there exists a closed derivation
D
in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L}
. Now
lemma

6.24
guarantees a scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
for
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
such that
L
is
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{D}}
-regular. Then
lemma

5.17
gives a scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\check{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathcal{D}}
for
\textit{Reg\/}^{+}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
such that
M=\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}
is
\check{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathcal{D}}
-regular. It follows that
M
is strongly regular.
∎

7
Binding–Capturing Chains

§ 7.1

(overview)
.

In this section we develop a characterisation for strong regularity of λ-terms by means of the ‘binding–capturing chains’ occurring in a term. This concept is related to the notions of scope and scope
+
as explained informally in
4.1
. Binding–capturing chains occur whereever scopes overlap; and they are fully contained within scopes
+
. First we give definitions for the concepts involved: binding, capturing, and binding–capturing chains. Then we show that strong regularity of regular λ-terms is equivalent to the absence of infinite binding–capturing chains.

§ 7.2

(binding and capturing)
.

We will define binding and capturing as relations on the positions of a λ-term. Binding relates an abstraction with the occurrences of the variable it binds. If
p
is the position of an abstraction (
λ{x}.\,{\dots}
) that abstracts over
x
and
q
is the position of an occurrence of
x
that is bound by the abstraction, then we will write
p\mathrel{\leftspoon}q
and say that
p
‘binds’
q
. Capturing relates an abstraction with variables that occur freely underneath the abstraction. If
p
is the position of an abstraction, and
q>p
is the position of a variable that is free in the entire subterm at position
p
, then we will write
p\mathrel{\dashleftarrow}q
and say that
p
‘captures’
q
. See
fig.

22
for an illustration of these concepts.

Figure 22:
The term graph of the term in
example

2.2
with its overlapping scopes (left), its nested scopes
+
(middle), and with its binding (
\mathrel{\leftspoon}
) and capturing (
\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}
) links (right).

§ 7.3

(positions in iCRS terms)
.

When we speak of positions in λ-terms (and thus iCRS terms) we act on the assumption that positions on iCRS terms are an established concept as for example in
[
31
]
. Note, however, that we deviate slightly from the scheme there in addressing the arguments of an
\mathsf{app}
by
0
and
1
instead of
1
and
2
.

§ 7.4

(binding–capturing chains in the literature)
.

Binding–capturing chains have been used in
[
15
]
to study α-avoiding rewrite sequences in a rewrite calculus for μ-unfolding. They originate from the notion of ‘gripping’ due to
[
40
]
, and from techniques developed in
[
46
]
concerning the notion of ‘holding’ of redexes (which is shown there as being ‘parting’ for CRSs, that is, never relating two residuals of the same redex).

We now define ‘binding’ and ‘capturing’ formally as binary relations on the set of positions of λ-terms.

Definition 7.5

(binding, capturing)
.

For every
M∈{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}}}\right)
we define the binary relations
\mathrel{\leftspoon}
and
\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}
on the set
{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)\subseteq ℕ^{*}
of positions of
M
:

(i)

We say that a binder at position
p

binds
a variable occurrence at position
q
, symbolically
p\mathrel{\leftspoon}q
, if
p
is a binder position, and
q
a variable position in
M
, and the binder at position
p
binds the variable occurrence at position
q
.

(ii)

We say that a variable occurrence at position
q

is captured by
a binder at position
p
, symbolically
q\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}p
(and that a binder at position
p

captures
a variable occurrence at position
q
, symbolically
p\mathrel{\dashleftarrow}q
), if
q
is a variable position and
p<q
a binder position in
M
, and there is no binder position
q_{0}
in
M
with
p≤q_{0}
and
q_{0}\mathrel{\leftspoon}q
.

Definition 7.6

(binding–capturing chain)
.

Let
M
be a λ-term. A finite or infinite sequence
\langle{p_{0},p_{1},p_{2},\dots}\rangle
in
{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
is called a
binding–capturing chain in
M
if
p_{0}
is the position of an abstraction in
M
, and the positions in the sequence are alternatingly linked via binding and capturing, starting with a binding:
p_{0}\mathrel{\leftspoon}p_{1}\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}p_{2}\mathrel{\leftspoon}\dots
.

§ 7.7
.

Binding–capturing chains are closely related to the notion of scope and scope
+
. In order to establish this, we first give precise definitions of the notions of scope and scope
+
in terms of an ‘in-scope’ rewrite relation on the positions of a λ-term: While the scope of a binder position
p
is the set of positions between
p
and variable positions bound at
p
(the positions directly reachable by a single ‘in-scope’ step), the scope
+
of
p
is the set of positions reachable by a finite number of successive ‘in-scope’ steps.

Notation 7.8

(binder positions)
.

In the following paragraphs for a given position
p
in some λ-term, we write
{\mathrm{bp}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p}}\right)
for the proposition ‘
p
is a binder position’.

Definition 7.9

(scope and scope
+
)
.

Let
M
be a λ-term. On the set
{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
of
M
, the
in-scope
relation
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{sc}}
(for
M
) is defined by:

p\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{sc}}q\penalty\ \Longleftrightarrow\penalty\ {\mathrm{bp}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p}}\right)\penalty\ ∧\penalty\ ∃p^{\prime}∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)\penalty\ \penalty\ p\mathrel{\leftspoon}^{=}p^{\prime}\>∧\>p≤q≤p^{\prime}

where
\mathrel{\leftspoon}^{=}
denotes the reflexive closure of the binding relation
\mathrel{\leftspoon}
.

For every binder position
p∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
, the
scope of
p
in
M
and the
scope
+
of
p
in
M
are defined as the following sets of positions in
M
:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{scope}}_{M}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p}}\right)

\displaystyle:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{q∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {p\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{sc}}q}}}\right\}

\displaystyle{\mathrm{scope^{+}\hskip-4.30554pt}}_{M}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p}}\right)

\displaystyle:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{q∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {p\mathrel{\to}^{+}_{\mathrm{sc}}q}}}\right\}

(Note that the scopes and scopes
+
of non-binder positions are empty sets.)

The following proposition establishes that binding–capturing chains starting at a binder position
p
span the positions of the scope
+
of
p
.

Proposition 7.10

(binding, capturing, and scope/scope
+
)
.

Let
M
be a λ-term and
p∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
be a binder position. Then for all positions
q∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
the following statements hold:

(i)

p\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{sc}}q\penalty\ ∧\penalty\ {\mathrm{bp}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q}}\right)\penalty\ \Longleftrightarrow\penalty\ p=q\penalty\ ∨\penalty\ p\mathrel{\leftspoon}⋅\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}q

(ii)

p\mathrel{\to}^{+}_{\mathrm{sc}}q\penalty\ \Longleftrightarrow\penalty\ ∃p^{\prime}∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)\penalty\ \penalty\ p\penalty\ (\mathrel{\leftspoon}⋅\mathrel{\dashrightarrow})^{*}\,⋅\,\mathrel{\leftspoon}^{=}p^{\prime}\>∧\>p≤q≤p^{\prime}

(iii)

{\mathrm{scope}}_{M}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p}}\right)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{q∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {∃p^{\prime}∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)\penalty\ \begin{array}[]{l}p\mathrel{\leftspoon}^{=}p^{\prime}\\
∧\>p≤q≤p^{\prime}\end{array}}}}\right\}

(iv)

{\mathrm{scope^{+}\hskip-4.30554pt}}_{M}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p}}\right)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{q∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {∃p^{\prime}∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)\penalty\ \begin{array}[]{l}p\penalty\ (\mathrel{\leftspoon}⋅\mathrel{\dashrightarrow})^{*}\,⋅\,\mathrel{\leftspoon}^{=}p^{\prime}\\
∧\>p≤q≤p^{\prime}\end{array}}}}\right\}

Conversely, every position that is covered by a binding–capturing chain starting at a binding position
p
is in the scope
+
of
p
:

Proposition 7.11
.

Let
\langle{p_{0},p_{1},p_{2},\dots}\rangle
be a binding–capturing chain in a λ-term
M
. Then it holds that
p_{0}<p_{2}<\dots
, and
p_{0}<p_{1}
,
p_{2}<p_{3}
,
\dots
. Furthermore, for all
q
with
p_{0}≤q≤p_{n}
for some
n∈ℕ
with
p_{n}
being a position on the chain it holds that
q∈{\mathrm{scope^{+}\hskip-4.30554pt}}_{M}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(p_{0}}}\right)
.

§ 7.12

(position-annotated variants
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}
and
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
)
.

In order to study the relationship between rewrite sequences in
\textit{{Reg}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
and binding–capturing chains we first introduce a position-annotated variant of
\textit{{Reg}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
.
The idea is, that when a prefixed term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y_{1}\dots y_{n}}}}}\right)\;{N}
is obtained as a generated subterm of a λ-term
M
by a
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg}}
or
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}
rewrite sequence
\tau
on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
, then in the position-annotated rewrite system a prefixed term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{y_{1},\dots,y_{n}}}}}\right)_{p_{1}\dots p_{n}}^{q}\;{N}
is obtained by an annotated version
\hat{\tau}
of the rewrite sequence
\tau
such that: the positions
p_{1},\dots,p_{n}
are the positions in (the original λ-term)
M
from which the bindings
λ{y_{1}}
,
\dots
,
λ{y_{n}}
in the abstraction prefix stem from; and
q
is the position in
M
of the body
N
of the subterm generated by
\tau
.

§ 7.13

(position-annotated decomposition in informal notation)
.

On
{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right)
we consider the following rewrite rules:

\displaystyle\varrho_{pos}^{@_{i}}:\penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)_{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}^{q}\;{{M_{0}}\;{M_{1}}}\mathrel{\to}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)_{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}^{qi}\;{M_{i}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\small($i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}$)}

\displaystyle\varrho_{pos}^{λ}:\penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)_{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}^{q}\;{λ{y}.\,{M_{0}}}\mathrel{\to}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n}y}}}}\right)_{p_{1},\dots,p_{n},q}^{q00}\;{M_{0}}

\displaystyle\varrho_{pos}^{\mathsf{S}}:\penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n+1}}}}}\right)_{p_{1},\dots,p_{n+1}}^{q}\;{M_{0}}\mathrel{\to}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)_{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}^{q}\;{M_{0}}

(if the binding
λ{x_{n+1}}
is vacuous)

\displaystyle\varrho_{pos}^{\mathrm{del}}:\penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n+1}}}}}\right)_{p_{1},\dots,p_{n+1}}^{q}\;{M_{0}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hskip 28.45274pt\mathrel{\to}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{i-1}x_{i+1}\dots x_{n+1}}}}}\right)_{p_{1},\dots p_{i-1},p_{i+1}\dots p_{n+1}}^{q}\;{M_{0}}

(if the binding
λ{x_{i}}
is vacuous)

Note that in the rule
\varrho_{pos}^{λ}
the position changes from
q
to
q00
, because of the underlying CRS notation for terms in
\boldsymbol{(λ)}
: when a term
{\mathsf{abs}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({[{y}]\hskip 1.0pt{M_{0}}}}}\right)
starts at position
q
, then the CRS-binding is at position
q0
, and the body
M_{0}
starts at position
q00
.

Definition 7.14

(position-annotated decomposition rewrite systems
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}^{\boldsymbol{-}}
,
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}
,
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
)
.

The CRS signature for
\boldsymbol{(λ)_{\mathrm{pos}}}
, the λ-calculus with position-annotated abstraction prefixes is given by
\Sigma^{\smash{(λ)}}_{\mathrm{pos}}=\Sigma^{λ}∪\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{{\mathsf{pre}}^{q}_{\langle{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}\rangle}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {p_{1},\dots,p_{n},q∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}^{*}}}}\right\}
where all of the function symbols
{\mathsf{pre}}^{q}_{\langle{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}\rangle}
are unary. We consider the following CRS rule schemes over
\Sigma^{\smash{(λ)}}_{\mathrm{pos}}
:

\displaystyle\varrho_{pos}^{@_{i}}:\penalty\ {{\mathsf{pre}}^{q}_{\langle{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}\rangle}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}]\hskip 1.0pt{{\mathsf{app}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({M_{0},\,M_{1}}}}\right)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hskip 28.45274pt\mathrel{\to}{{\mathsf{pre}}^{qi}_{\langle{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}\rangle}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}]\hskip 1.0pt{M_{i}}}}\right)\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\small($i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}$)}

\displaystyle\varrho_{pos}^{λ}:\penalty\ {{\mathsf{pre}}^{q}_{\langle{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}\rangle}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}]\hskip 1.0pt{{\mathsf{abs}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({[{y}]\hskip 1.0pt{M_{0}}}}}\right)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hskip 28.45274pt\mathrel{\to}{{\mathsf{pre}}^{q00}_{\langle{p_{1},\dots,p_{n},q}\rangle}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{x_{1}\dots x_{n}y}]\hskip 1.0pt{M_{0}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\varrho_{pos}^{\mathsf{S}}:\penalty\ {{\mathsf{pre}}^{q}_{\langle{p_{1},\dots,p_{n+1}}\rangle}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{x_{1}\dots x_{n+1}}]\hskip 1.0pt{M_{0}}}}\right)\mathrel{\to}{{\mathsf{pre}}^{q}_{\langle{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}\rangle}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}]\hskip 1.0pt{M_{0}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\varrho_{pos}^{\mathrm{del}}:\penalty\ {{\mathsf{pre}}^{q}_{\langle{p_{1},\dots,p_{n+1}}\rangle}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{x_{1}\dots x_{n+1}}]\hskip 1.0pt{M_{0}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hskip 28.45274pt\mathrel{\to}{{\mathsf{pre}}^{q}_{\langle{p_{1},\dots p_{i-1},p_{i+1}\dots p_{n+1}}\rangle}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left([{x_{1}\dots x_{i-1}x_{i+1}\dots x_{n+1}}]\hskip 1.0pt{M_{0}}}}\right)

By
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}^{\boldsymbol{-}}
we denote the CRS with the rules
\varrho_{pos}^{@_{i}}
and
\varrho_{pos}^{λ}
. By
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}
/
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
we denote the CRS consisting of the rules
\varrho_{pos}^{@_{i}}
,
\varrho_{pos}^{λ}
, and
\varrho_{pos}^{\mathrm{del}}
/
\varrho_{pos}^{\mathsf{S}}
.

By
\textit{Reg}_{\textit{pos}}^{-}
,
\textit{Reg}_{\textit{pos}}
and
\textit{Reg}_{\textit{pos}}^{+}
we denote the ARSs induced by the iCRSs derived from
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}^{\boldsymbol{-}}
,
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}
,
\textit{{Reg}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, restricted to position-annotated terms in
{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\right)
.

Proposition 7.15

(position-annotated rewrite sequences)
.

It holds:

(i)

Every rewrite sequence

\tau:\penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{0}}}}}\right)\;{M_{0}}\mathrel{\to}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{1}}}}}\right)\;{M_{1}}\mathrel{\to}\dots\mathrel{\to}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{n}}}}}\right)\;{M_{n}}

(2)

in
\textit{Reg}^{-}
,
Reg
, or
\textit{Reg}^{+}
can be transformed (lifted) step by step, for a given
q_{0}∈ℕ^{*}
and
\vec{p}_{0}∈\vec{ℕ}^{*}
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{p}_{0}}}}\right|=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}_{0}}}}\right|
, by adding these and appropriate further position annotations
q_{1},\dots,q_{n}∈ℕ^{*}
and
\vec{p}_{1},\dots,\vec{p}_{n}∈\vec{ℕ^{*}}
, to a rewrite sequence:

\hat{\tau}:\penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{0}}}}}\right)_{\vec{p}_{0}}^{q_{0}}\;{M_{0}}\mathrel{\to}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{1}}}}}\right)_{\vec{p}_{1}}^{q_{1}}\;{M_{1}}\mathrel{\to}\dots\mathrel{\to}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{n}}}}}\right)_{\vec{p}_{n}}^{q_{n}}\;{M_{n}}

(3)

in
\textit{Reg}_{\textit{pos}}^{-}
,
\textit{Reg}_{\textit{pos}}
, or
\textit{Reg}_{\textit{pos}}^{+}
, accordingly, such that the result of dropping the position annotations in the prefix of
\hat{\tau}
is again
\tau
.

(ii)

Conversely, every rewrite sequence
\xi
in
\textit{Reg}_{\textit{pos}}^{-}
,
\textit{Reg}_{\textit{pos}}
, or
\textit{Reg}_{\textit{pos}}^{+}
of the form
eq.

3
can be transformed step by step, by dropping the position annotations in the prefix, to a rewrite sequence
\check{\xi}
of the form
eq.

3
in
\textit{Reg}^{-}
,
Reg
, or
\textit{Reg}^{+}
, respectively.

The transformations in
item

i
and
item

ii
preserve eagerness/laziness of rewrite sequences.

The proposition below characterises the binding relation
\mathrel{\leftspoon}
and the capturing relation
\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}
on the positions of an
infinite term
M
with the help of rewrite sequences with respect to
\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{-}}}

on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)^{\epsilon}\;{M}
in
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}^{\boldsymbol{-}}
down to ‘variable occurrences’
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)_{\vec{p}}^{q}\;{x_{i}}
in
M
.

Proposition 7.16

(binding, capturing, and
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}^{\boldsymbol{-}}
rewrite sequences)
.

For all
M∈{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}}}\right)
and positions
p,q∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
it holds:

\displaystyle p\mathrel{\leftspoon}q

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\begin{tabular}[]{@{}l@{}}there is a rewrite sequence $\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{}}}\right\rangle}^{\epsilon}\;{M}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathrm{reg^{-}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)_{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}^{q}\;{x_{i}}$\\
with $x_{1}\dots x_{n}$ distinct, $i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{1,\dots,n}}}\right\}$, such that $p=p_{i}$\end{tabular}

\displaystyle q\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}p

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\begin{tabular}[]{@{}l@{}}there is a rewrite sequence $\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{}}}\right\rangle}^{\epsilon}\;{M}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathrm{reg^{-}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)_{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}^{q}\;{x_{i}}$\\
with $x_{1}\dots x_{n}$ distinct, $i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{1,\dots,n}}}\right\}$, such that $p∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{p_{i+1},\dots,p_{n}}}}\right\}$\end{tabular}

The following lemmas describe the close relationship between, on the one hand,
binding–capturing chains in a λ-term
M
, and on the other hand,

\mathrel{\to}_{\mathrm{reg^{+}}}
-rewrite-sequences on
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)^{\epsilon}\;{M}

in
\textit{{Reg}}_{\textit{{pos}}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
that are guided by the eager scope
+
-delimiting strategy.

Lemma 7.17

(binding–capturing chains)
.

For all
M∈{\mathrm{{Ter}^{\infty}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{λ}}}\right)
the following statements hold:

(i)

If there is a rewriting sequence of the form

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)^{\epsilon}\;{M}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{0}\dots x_{n_{1}}}}}}\right)_{p_{0},\dots,p_{n_{1}}}^{q}\;{N}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{0}\dots x_{n_{1}}\dots x_{n_{2}}}}}}\right)_{p_{0},\dots,p_{n_{1}},\dots,p_{n_{2}}}^{q^{\prime}}\;{O}

then there exist
q_{n_{1}+1},\dots,q_{n_{2}}∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
such that
p_{n_{1}}\mathrel{\leftspoon}q_{n_{1}+1}\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}p_{n_{1}+1}\mathrel{\leftspoon}\dots\mathrel{\leftspoon}q_{n_{2}}\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}p_{n_{2}}
.

(ii)

If
p_{0}\mathrel{\leftspoon}q_{1}\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}p_{1}\mathrel{\leftspoon}\dots\mathrel{\leftspoon}q_{n}\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}p_{n}
is a binding–capturing chain in
M
, then there exist
r_{0},\dots,r_{m},s∈{\mathrm{Pos}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
with
m≥n
such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)^{\epsilon}\;{M}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{0}\dots x_{m}}}}}\right)_{r_{0},\dots,r_{m}}^{s}\;{N}
and furthermore
p_{0},\dots,p_{n}∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{r_{0},\dots,r_{m}}}}\right\}
such that
p_{0}<p_{1}<\dots<p_{n}=r_{m}
.

Lemma 7.18

(length of binding–capturing chains)
.

Let
M
be a λ-term such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{0}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)\;{N}
. Then
M
contains a binding–capturing chain of length
n
.

Proof.

By
proposition

7.15

item

i
, the assumed rewrite sequence
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{0}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)\;{N}
in
\textit{Reg}^{+}
can be lifted to a rewrite sequence
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)^{\epsilon}\;{M}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{0}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)_{p_{0},\dots,p_{n}}^{q}\;{N}
in
\textit{Reg}_{\textit{pos}}^{+}
. Then by
lemma

7.17

item

i
, there exists a binding–capturing chain of length
n
.
∎

§ 7.19

(binding–capturing chains and scope/scope
+
)
.

The notion of scope and scope
+
helps to understand the relationship between binding–capturing chains and rewrite sequences in
\textit{{Reg}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
. A binding–capturing chain corresponds to the overlap of scopes, or in other words the nesting of scopes
+
. An infinite binding–capturing chain thus corresponds to a infinitely deep nesting of scopes
+
and therefore to an unrestricted growth of the prefix in certain rewriting sequences in
\textit{{Reg}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
.

Lemma 7.20

(infinite binding–capturing chains)
.

Let
M
be a λ-term, and let
\tau
be an infinite rewrite sequence in
Reg
w.r.t. the eager scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}
:

\tau\penalty\ :\penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{0}}}}}\right)\;{M_{0}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{1}}}}}\right)\;{M_{1}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\dots\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{i}}}}}\right)\;{M_{i}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\dots

Furthermore suppose that for
{\mathrm{p}}:ℕ\textrightarrow ℕ
,
i↦{\mathrm{p}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right):=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}_{i}}}}\right|
, the prefix length function associated with
\tau
, there exists a lower bound
{\mathrm{lb}}:ℕ\textrightarrow ℕ
such
that
{\mathrm{lb}}
is non-decreasing, and
\lim_{n\textrightarrow\infty}{\mathrm{lb}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n}}\right)=\infty
.
Then there exists an infinite binding–capturing chain in
M
.

Proof.

Let
M
,
\tau
,
{\mathrm{p}}
,
{\mathrm{lb}}
as above. By
proposition

7.15

item

i
,
\tau
can be lifted to position annotated counterpart

\hat{\tau}:\;\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)^{\epsilon}\;{M}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{0}}}}}\right)^{\epsilon}\;{M_{0}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{1}}}}}\right)_{\vec{p}_{1}}^{q_{1}}\;{M_{1}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\dots\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{i}}}}}\right)_{\vec{p}_{i}}^{q_{i}}\;{M_{i}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\dots

where, for all
i∈ℕ
,
q_{i}
are positions and
\vec{p}_{i}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{p_{1},\dots,p_{m_{i}}}}}\right\rangle
vectors of positions, with
m_{i}∈ℕ
.

Next we define the function:

{\mathrm{st}}:ℕ\textrightarrow ℕ,\quad l↦{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(l}}\right):=\min{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{i}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {{\mathrm{lb}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)≥l}}}\right\}}

which is well-defined, since
\lim_{n\textrightarrow\infty}{{\mathrm{lb}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n}}\right)=\infty}
. It describes a prefix stabilisation property: for every
l∈ℕ
, it gives the first index
i={\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(l}}\right)
with the property that the prefix of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{i}}}}}\right)\;{M_{i}}
contains more than
l
abstractions, and (since
{\mathrm{lb}}
is non-decreasing, and a lower bound for
{\mathrm{p}}
) that from
i
onward the
l
-th abstraction never disappears again, for
j≥i
, in terms
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{j}}}}}\right)\;{M_{j}}
that follow in
\tau
as well as in
\hat{\tau}
. Furthermore,
{\mathrm{st}}
is non-decreasing, as an easy consequence of its definition, and unbounded: if
{\mathrm{st}}
were bounded by
M∈ℕ
, then
∀l∈ℕ\penalty\ ∃i∈ℕ\penalty\ i≤M∧{\mathrm{lb}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)≥l
would follow, which cannot be the case since
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{{\mathrm{lb}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(0}}\right),\dots,{\mathrm{lb}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)}}}\right\}
is a finite set. By non-decreasingness and unboundedness it also follows that
\lim_{n\textrightarrow\infty}{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n}}\right)=\infty}
.

So when the rewrite sequence
\hat{\tau}
is split into segments indicated in

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)^{\epsilon}\;{M}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\dots

\displaystyle\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}}}}\right)_{\vec{p}_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}^{q_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}\;{M_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i+1}}\right)}}}}}\right)_{\vec{p}_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i+1}}\right)}}^{q_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i+1}}\right)}}\;{M_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i+1}}\right)}}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\dots

then it follows that all terms of the sequence after
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}}}}\right)_{\vec{p}_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}^{q_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}\;{M_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}
have an abstraction prefix of length greater or equal to
i
, for all
i∈ℕ
.

Now note that in a step

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{p_{1},\dots,p_{n}}}}\right\rangle}^{q}\;{N}\mathrel{\to}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}^{\prime}}}}}\right)_{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{p^{\prime}_{1},\dots,p^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}}}}}\right\rangle}^{q^{\prime}}\;{N^{\prime}}

in
\textit{Reg}_{\textit{pos}}
that does not shorten the abstraction prefix it holds that
n≤n^{\prime}
and

p^{\prime}_{1}=p_{1}
,
\dots
,
p^{\prime}_{n}=p_{n}
,
that is, positions in the vector in the subscript of the abstraction prefix are preserved.
As a consequence it follows for the rewrite sequence
\hat{\tau}
that for all
i∈ℕ
and
j>i
the position vector
\vec{p}_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}
in the term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}}}}\right)_{\vec{p}_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}^{q_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\;{M_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}
is of the form

\vec{p}_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{p_{1,{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)},\dots,p_{i,{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)},p_{j,{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)},\dots,p_{j,m_{j}}}}}\right\rangle.

This implies furthermore that for all
i∈ℕ
:

\vec{p}_{{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{p_{1,{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1}}\right)},p_{2,{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2}}\right)},\dots,p_{i,{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)},\dots,p_{i,m_{i}}}}}\right\rangle.

Then
lemma

7.17

item

i
,
implies the existence of positions
q_{2},q_{3},\dots
such that

p_{1,{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1}}\right)}\mathrel{\leftspoon}q_{2}\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}p_{2,{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2}}\right)}\mathrel{\leftspoon}q_{3}\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}\dots\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}p_{i,{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}\mathrel{\leftspoon}q_{i+1}\mathrel{\dashrightarrow}p_{i+1,{\mathrm{st}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i+1}}\right)}\mathrel{\leftspoon}\dots

and thereby, an infinite binding–capturing chain in
M
.
∎

Now we formulate and prove the main theorem of this section, which applies the concept of binding–capturing chain to pin down, among all λ-terms that are regular, those that are strongly regular.

Theorem 7.21

(binding–capturing chains and strong regularity)
.

A regular λ-term is strongly regular if and only if it contains only finite binding–capturing chains.

And taking into account
proposition

4.43

item

i
, we obtain:

Corollary 7.22
.

A λ-term is strongly regular if and only if it is regular and contains only finite binding–capturing chains.

Proof of
theorem

7.21
.

Let
M
be a λ-term that is regular.

For showing the implication “⇒”, we assume that
M
is also strongly regular. Then there exists a scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}
such that
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
is finite. By
proposition

4.44

item

i
it follows that then also
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
is finite for the eager scope
+
-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}
in
\textit{Reg}^{+}
. Now let
n
be the longest abstraction prefix of a term in
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
. Then it follows by
lemma

7.18
that the length of every binding–capturing chain in
M
is bounded by
n-1
. Hence
M
only contains finite binding–capturing chains.

In the rest of this proof, we establish the implication “⇐” in the statement of the theorem. For this we argue indirectly: assuming that
M
is not strongly regular, we show the existence of an infinite binding–capturing chain in
M
.

So suppose that
M
is not strongly regular. Then for all scope
+
-delimiting strategies
\mathbb{S}
in
\textit{Reg}^{+}
it holds that
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
is infinite. This means that in particular
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
is infinite for the eager scope-delimiting strategy
\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}
on
\textit{Reg}^{+}
. It follows that the number of
\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}
-reducts, and hence the generated sub-ARS
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}}}\right)
of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
in
\textit{Reg}^{+}
is infinite. Since
\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}
on
\textit{Reg}^{+}
has branching degree
≤2
(branching actually only happens at sources of
\mathrel{\to}_{@_{i}}
-steps), it follows by Kőnig’s Lemma that there exists an infinite rewrite sequence:

\tau:\penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{0}}}}}\right)\;{M_{0}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\dots\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{i}}}}}\right)\;{M_{i}}\mathrel{\to}_{\mathbb{S}^{+}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\dots

(4)

in
\textit{Reg}^{+}
that passes through distinct terms. By
lemma

4.29

item

i
, this rewrite sequence projects to a rewrite sequence:

\check{\tau}:\penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}^{\prime}_{0}}}}}\right)\;{M_{0}}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\dots\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}^{\prime}_{i}}}}}\right)\;{M_{i}}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\dots

(5)

in
Reg
in the sense that:

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{i}}}}}\right)\;{M_{i}}\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathrm{del}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}^{\prime}_{i}}}}}\right)\;{M_{i}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\small(for all $i∈ℕ$)}.

(6)

Note that, in the terms, the projection merely shortens the length of the abstraction prefix. Since
M
is regular,
{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)
is finite by
proposition

4.44

item

i
, and hence it follows that only finitely many terms occur in
\check{\tau}
.

Now we will use this contrast with
\tau
, and the fact that the terms of
\tau
project to terms in
\check{\tau}
via
\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathrm{del}}
-prefix compression rewrite sequences, to show that the prefix lengths in terms of
\tau
are unbounded, and stronger still, that these lengths actually tend to infinity. More precisely, we show the following:

∀l∈ℕ\penalty\ ∃i_{0}∈ℕ\penalty\ ∀i≥i_{0}\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}_{i}}}}\right|≥l

(7)

Suppose that this statement does not hold. Then there exists
l_{0}∈ℕ
such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}_{i}}}}\right|<l_{0}
for infinitely many
i∈ℕ
. This means that there is an increasing sequence
i_{0}<i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<\dots
in ℕ such that:

\displaystyle S:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{i_{j}}}}}}\right)\;{M_{i_{j}}}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {j∈ℕ}}}\right\}\text{ is infinite}

(8)

and for all
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}_{i_{j}}}}}}\right)\;{M_{i_{j}}}∈S\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}_{i_{j}}}}}\right|<l_{0}

(9)

(
S
is infinite since the terms in
\tau
are distinct). On the other hand we have:

\displaystyle T:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}^{\prime}_{i_{j}}}}}}\right)\;{M_{i_{j}}}}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {j∈ℕ}}}\right\}\;\subseteq\;{\mathrm{ST}}_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{eag}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M}}\right)\text{ is finite}

(10)

because
M
is regular. However, since every term in
S
has a
\mathrel{\twoheadrightarrow}_{\mathrm{del}}
-reduct in
T
due to
eq.

6
, as well as an abstraction prefix of a length bounded by
l_{0}
, it follows by
proposition

4.26

item

ii
, that
S
also has to be finite, conflicting with
eq.

8
. We have reached a contradiction, and thereby established
eq.

7
.

Now we are able to define a lower bound on the lengths of the prefixes in
\tau
that fulfils the requirements of
lemma

7.20
. We define the function:

{\mathrm{lb}}:ℕ\textrightarrow ℕ,\quad n↦{\mathrm{lb}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n}}\right):=\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}_{n^{\prime}}}}}\right|}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {n^{\prime}≥n}}}\right\}

Its definition guarantees that
{\mathrm{lb}}
is a lower bound on the prefix lengths in
\tau
, and that
{\mathrm{lb}}
is non-decreasing. Furthermore also
\lim_{n\textrightarrow\infty}{\mathrm{lb}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n}}\right)=\infty
follows by non-decreasingness, in addition to unboundedness of
{\mathrm{lb}}
: for arbitrary
l∈ℕ
, by
eq.

7
there exists
n_{0}∈ℕ
such that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}_{n}}}}\right|≥l
holds for all
n∈ℕ
,
n≥n_{0}
; this entails
{\mathrm{lb}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n_{0}}}\right)≥l
.

Now since
M
,
\tau
, together with
{\mathrm{lb}}
as defined above, satisfy the assumptions of
lemma

7.20
, this lemma can be applied, yielding an infinite binding–capturing chain in
M
.
∎

Example 7.23

(infinite binding–capturing chain)
.

The infinite λ-term from
example

2.2
with a representation as a higher-order recursive program scheme in
example

4.46
, which was recognised there to be regular but not strongly regular, possesses an infinite binding–capturing chain as indicated on the right in
fig.

22
.

8
Expressibility by terms in
\boldsymbol{λ}_{\mathsf{letrec}}

§ 8.1

(overview)
.

In this section we finish the proof of our main characterisation result: we prove that every strongly regular λ-term is
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-expressible. For this purpose we introduce an annotated variant of one of the proof systems for strongly regular λ-terms. We show that every closed derivation in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
, which witnesses that
M
is strongly regular, can be annotated, by adding appropriate
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms to each prefixed term in the derivation, into a derivation in the annotated system with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{L\,:\,M}
such that the
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term annotation
L
expresses the λ-term
M
. We show the correctness of this construction by transforming the derivation in the annotated proof system into a derivation in the proof system
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\boldsymbol{=}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.1pt{\boldsymbol{λ}}}}
=
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({}}}\right)\;{M}
, and then drawing upon the soundness of
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\boldsymbol{=}}
with respect to equality of strongly regular λ-terms.

\mathsf{0}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}y}}}}\right)\;{y\,:\,y}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}y}}}}\right)\;{L\,:\,M}

λ

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{λ{y}.\,{L}\,:\,λ{y}.\,{M}}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{L_{0}\,:\,M_{0}}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{L_{1}\,:\,M_{1}}

@

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{{L_{0}}\;{L_{1}}\,:\,{M_{0}}\;{M_{1}}}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n-1}}}}}\right)\;{L\,:\,M}

\mathsf{S}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\small(if the binding $λ{x_{n}}$ is vacuous)}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x_{1}\dots x_{n}}}}}\right)\;{L\,:\,M}

[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{u}\,:\,M}]^{u}

\mathcal{D}_{0}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{{L}[{u}:={\mathsf{c}_{u}}]\,:\,M}

\mathrm{FIX},u\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\small\begin{tabular}[]{@{}l@{}}(if $0pt{\mathcal{D}_{0}}≥1$, and $\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{y}}}}\right|≥\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}}}}\right|$ for all\\
$\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}$ on threads in $\mathcal{D}_{0}$ from open\\
assumptions $(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{u\,:\,M})^{u}$ down)\end{tabular}}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{(\mathsf{let}\;{u=L}\;\mathsf{in}\;{u})\,:\,M}

Figure 23:
Annotated natural-deduction style proof system
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
for strongly regular λ-terms, a version of
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms as annotations.

We start by introducing a variant of the proof system
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
in which the formulas are closed, prefixed,
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term-annotated λ-terms.

Definition 8.2

(the proof system
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
)
.

The formulas of the proof system
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
are closed expressions of the form
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{L\,:\,M}
with
\vec{x}
a variable prefix vector,
λ{\vec{x}}.\,{L}
a
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term, and
λ{\vec{x}}.\,{M}
a λ-term. The axioms and rules of
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
are annotated versions of the axioms and rules of the proof system
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
from
definition

6.4
and
fig.

15
, and are displayed in
fig.

23
.

Remark 8.3
.

For an example that illustrates why we have chosen to formulate an annotated version only of the proof system
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, but not of
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, please see
example

8.6
.

The following proposition is a statement that is entirely analogous to
proposition

6.7
.

Proposition 8.4

(cycles are guarded)
.

For all for all instances
\iota
of the rule
\mathrm{FIX}
in a derivation
D
(possibly with open assumptions) in
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
it holds: every thread from
\iota
upwards to a marked assumption that is discharged at
\iota
passes at least one instance of a rule
(λ)
or
(@)
.

The lemma below states a straightforward connection between derivations in

\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
and derivations in its annotated version
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
.

Lemma 8.5

(from
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
- to
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-derivations, and back)
.

The following transformations are possible between derivations in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
and derivations in
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
:

(i)

Every derivation
D
in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}
can be transformed into a derivation
\hat{\mathcal{D}}
in
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{L\,:\,M}
such that there is a bijective correspondence between marked assumptions
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{M})^{u}
in
D
and marked assumptions
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{u\,:\,M})^{u}
in
\hat{\mathcal{D}}
. (As a consequence,
\hat{\mathcal{D}}
is a closed derivation if
D
is closed.) More precisely,
\hat{\mathcal{D}}
can be obtained from
D
by replacing every term occurrence
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
by an occurrence of
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{P\,:\,N}
for a prefixed
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{P}
with the property that every prefix variable
y_{i}
bound in
P
is also bound in
N
. Thereby occurrences of marked assumptions and axioms
\mathsf{0}
in
D
give rise to occurrences of marked assumptions and axioms
\mathsf{0}
in
\hat{\mathcal{D}}
, respectively; instances of the
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-rules λ, @,
\mathsf{S}
, and
\mathrm{FIX}
in
D
give rise to instances of
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
-rules λ, @,
\mathsf{S}
, and
\mathrm{FIX}
in
\hat{\mathcal{D}}
, respectively.

(ii)

From every closed derivation
D
in
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{L\,:\,M}
a closed derivation
\check{\mathcal{D}}
in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with conclusion
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{M}
can be obtained by dropping the annotations with
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms.

Proof.

Statement
item

i
of the lemma can be established through a proof by induction on the depth 0pt
D
of a derivation
D
in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with possibly open assumptions. In the base case, axioms (
\mathsf{0}
) of
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
are annotated to axioms (
\mathsf{0}
) of
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, and marked assumptions
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N})^{u}
in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
to marked assumptions
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{u}\,:\,N})^{u}
. In the induction step it has to be shown that a derivation
D
in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
with immediate subderivation
\mathcal{D}_{0}
can be annotated to a derivation
\hat{\mathcal{D}}
in
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
, using the induction hypothesis which guarantees that an annotated version
\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
has already been obtained.
Then for obtaining
\hat{\mathcal{D}}
from
\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
the fact is used that the rules in
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
uniquely determine the annotation in the conclusion of an instance once the annotation(s) in the premise(s) (and in the case of
\mathrm{FIX}
additionally the annotation markers used in the assumptions that are discharged) are given. In order to establish that instances of
\mathsf{S}
in
D
give rise to corresponding instances of
\mathsf{S}
in
\hat{\mathcal{D}}
, the part of the induction hypothesis is used which guarantees that the
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term annotation in the premise contains not more variable bindings than the λ-term it annotates.

Statement
item

ii
of the lemma is a consequence of the fact that, by dropping the
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term-annotations, every instance of a rule of
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
give rise to an instance of the corresponding rule in
\mathbf{Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
. Formally the statement can again be established by induction on the depth of derivations in
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
.
∎

.

:

(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{x}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{u}\,:\,λ{y}.\,{{{M}\;{y}}\;{x}}})^{u}

λ

\iota,u

Observe that, equally as was the case for
\mathcal{D}_{r}
, also in

there occurs, on the thread between the marked assumption at the top and the rule instance

at which this assumption is discharged, a formula, namely

as well as in the assumption. Thus

is not an instance of the rule

in
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
.

Furthermore note that the
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term extracted by

does not unfold to
M
, and hence does not express
M
. This example shows that the side-condition on instances of
\mathrm{FIX}
in
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
cannot be weakened to the form used for the rule
\mathrm{FIX}
in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
when the aim is to extract a
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-term that unfolds to the infinite λ-term in the conclusion.

The central property of the proof system
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
still remains to be shown:
that the
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms in the conclusion of a derivation in this system
does actually unfold to the infinite λ-term in the conclusion.
This will be established below in
lemma

8.11
and
theorem

8.12
.
But as an intermediary proof system that will allow us to use results about the
proof system
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
from
section

6
, we also introduce an annotated
version of the rule
\mathsf{letrec}
in
\mathbf{Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
, and an according annotated proof system.

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{f_{i}}\,:\,M_{i}}]}}}\right\}_{i=1,\dots,n}

\mathcal{D}_{j}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\dots\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{{L_{j}}[{\vec{f}}:={\vec{\mathsf{c}}_{\vec{f}}}]\,:\,M_{j}}\dots}}}\right\}_{j=0,\dots,n}

\mathrm{\mathrm{FIX}_{\mathsf{letrec}}}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{(\mathsf{let}\;{f_{1}=L_{1}\dots f_{n}=L_{n}}\;\mathsf{in}\;{L_{0}})\,:\,M_{0}}

where
\mathsf{c}_{f_{1}},\dots,\mathsf{c}_{f_{n}}
are distinct constants fresh for
L_{0},\dots,L_{n}
, and substitutions
{L_{l}}[{\vec{f}}:={\vec{\mathsf{c}}_{\vec{f}}}]
stands for
L_{l}[f_{1}:=\mathsf{c}_{f_{1}},\dots,f_{n}:=\mathsf{c}_{f_{n}}]
.

side-conditions
:
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{y}}}}\right|≥\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|{\vec{x}}}}\right|
holds for the prefix length of every
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{y}}}}}\right)\;{N}
on a thread in
\mathcal{D}_{j}
for
0≤j≤n
from an open assumptions
(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({λ{\vec{x}}}}}\right)\;{\mathsf{c}_{f_{i}}})^{u_{i}}
downwards; for bottommost instances: the arising derivation is guarded on access path cycles.

Figure 24:
The proof system
\mathbf{ann-Reg}^{\boldsymbol{+}}_{\mathsf{letrec}}
for
λ_{\mathsf{letrec}}
-terms arises from the proof system
\mathbf{ann-Reg}_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\boldsymbol{+}}
(see
