is a morphism from the structure
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V_{1},{\mathrm{lab}}_{1},{\mathrm{args}}_{1},r_{1},{\mathrm{Sc}}_{1}}}}\right\rangle
to the structure
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V_{2},{\mathrm{lab}}_{2},{\mathrm{args}}_{2},r_{2},{\mathrm{Sc}}_{2}}}}\right\rangle
, i.e. a function
h:V_{1}\textrightarrow V_{2}
such that
h
is a homomorphism from
G_{1}
to
G_{2}
, the term graphs underlying
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{2}
respectively, and additionally, for all
v∈{\mathrm{V}}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)

\bar{h}({\mathrm{Sc}}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right))={\mathrm{Sc}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(h(v)}}\right)

(1)

where
\bar{h}
is the homomorphic extension of
h
to sets over
V_{1}
, i.e.
\bar{h}:\powerset(V_{1})\textrightarrow\powerset(V_{2})
,
A↦\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{h(a)}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {a∈A}}}\right\}
.

If there exists a homomorphism
h
from
\mathcal{G}_{1}
to
\mathcal{G}_{2}
, then we write
\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{h}\mathcal{G}_{2}
or
\mathcal{G}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\leftarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{h}\mathcal{G}_{1}
, or, dropping
h
as subscript,
\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}\mathcal{G}_{2}
or
\mathcal{G}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\leftarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}\mathcal{G}_{1}
.

Definition 4.11

(isomorphism)
.

An
isomorphism
from
\mathcal{G}_{1}
to
\mathcal{G}_{2}
is a homomorphism from
\mathcal{G}_{1}
to
\mathcal{G}_{2}
that, as a function from
V_{1}
to
V_{2}
, is bijective.

If there exists an isomorphism
i:V_{1}\textrightarrow V_{2}
from
\mathcal{G}_{1}
to
\mathcal{G}_{2}
, then we say that
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{2}
are
isomorphic
, and write
\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}_{i}\mathcal{G}_{2}
or simply
\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}\mathcal{G}_{2}
. The property of existence of an isomorphism between two λ-ho-term-graphs forms an equivalence relation. We denote by
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}}}^{\boldsymbol{λ}}_{0}
and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}}}^{\boldsymbol{λ}}_{1}
the isomorphism equivalence classes of λ-ho-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{0}}
and
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{1}}
, respectively.

Definition 4.12

(bisimulation)
.

A
bisimulation
between
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{2}
is a (λ-ho-term-graph-like) structure
\mathcal{G}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{R,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r,{\mathrm{Sc}}}}}\right\rangle
where
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{R,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r}}}\right\rangle∈{\mathrm{TG^{-}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Sigma}}\right)
,
R\subseteq V_{1}\times V_{2}
and
r=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{r_{1},r_{2}}}}\right\rangle
such that
\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\leftarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{\pi_{1}}\mathcal{G}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{\pi_{2}}\mathcal{G}_{2}
where
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
are projection functions, defined, for
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{1,2}}}\right\}
, by
\pi_{i}:V_{1}\times V_{2}\textrightarrow V_{i}
,
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{v_{1},v_{2}}}}\right\rangle↦v_{i}
.
If there a bisimulation
R
exists between
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{2}
, then we write
\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\leftrightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{R}\mathcal{G}_{2}
, or just
\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\leftrightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}\mathcal{G}_{2}
.

5
Abstraction-prefix based λ-ho-term-graphs

§ 5.1

(overview)
.

By an ‘abstraction-prefix based λ-higher-order-term-graph’ we will mean a term-graph over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
for
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
that is endowed with a correct abstraction prefix function. Such a function
{\mathrm{P}}
maps abstraction vertices
w
to words
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)
consisting of all those abstraction vertices that have
w
in their scope
+
, in the order of their nesting from outermost to innermost abstraction vertex. If
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)=v_{1}\dots v_{n}
, then
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
are the abstraction vertices that have
w
in their scope, with
v_{1}
the outermost and
v_{n}
the innermost such abstraction vertex.

§ 5.2

(comparison to λ-ho-term-graphs)
.

So the conceptual difference between the scope functions of λ-ho-term-graphs defined in the previous section, and abstraction-prefix functions of the λ-ap-ho-term-graphs defined below is the following: A scope function
{\mathrm{Sc}}
associates with every abstraction vertex
v
the information on its scope
+

{\mathrm{Sc}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)
and makes it available at
v
. In contrast, an abstraction-prefix function
{\mathrm{P}}
gathers all the scoping information that is relevant to a vertex
v
(in the sense that it contains all abstraction vertices in whose scope
v
is) and makes it available at
v
in the form
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)
. The fact that abstraction-prefix functions make relevant scope information locally available at all vertices leads to simpler correctness conditions.

Definition 5.3

(abstraction-prefix function for
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
-term-graphs)
.

Let
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
and
G=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r}}}\right\rangle
be a
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
-term-graph. A function
{\mathrm{P}}:V\textrightarrow V^{*}
from vertices of
G
to words of vertices is called an
abstraction-prefix function
for
G
. Such a function is called
correct
if for all
v,w∈V
and
k∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
:

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(r}}\right)=\epsilon

(root)

\displaystyle v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)\;∧\;v\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}w\penalty\ ⇒

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)≤{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)v

(λ)

\displaystyle v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(@}}\right)\;∧\;v\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}w\penalty\ ⇒

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)≤{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)

(@)

\displaystyle v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{0}}}\right)\penalty\ ⇒

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)≠\epsilon

(
\mathsf{0}_{0}
)

\displaystyle v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{0}}}\right)\;∧\;v\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}w\penalty\ ⇒

\displaystyle\penalty\ w∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)\;∧\;{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)\,w={\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)

(
\mathsf{0}_{1}
)

Analogous to
definition

4.6
, if
i=0
, then
eq.

0
0

is trivially true and hence superfluous, and if
i=1
, then
eq.

0
1

is redundant, because it follows from
eq.

0
1

.

We say that
G

admits
a correct abstraction-prefix function if such a function exists for
G
.

Definition 5.4

(λ-ap-ho-term-graph)
.

Let
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
. A
λ-ap-ho-term-graph
(short for
abstraction-prefix based λ-higher-order-term-graph
) over signature
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
is a tuple
\mathcal{G}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r,{\mathrm{P}}}}}\right\rangle
where
G_{\mathcal{G}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r}}}\right\rangle
is a
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
-term-graph, called the term graph
underlying

G
, and
{\mathrm{P}}
is a correct abstraction-prefix function for
G_{\mathcal{G}}
. The classes of λ-ap-ho-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
will be denoted by
\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\smash{(λ)}}
.

Example 5.5
.

See
fig.

2
for two λ-ap-ho-term-graphs, which correspond (see
example

5.12
) to the λ-ho-term-graphs in
fig.

1
.

\mathcal{G}-0^{\prime}
:

\mathcal{G}-1^{\prime}
:

Figure 2:
The λ-ap-ho-term-graphs corresponding to the λ-ho-term-graphs in
fig.

1
. The subscripts of abstraction vertices indicate their names. The super-scripts of vertices indicate their abstraction-prefixes.

The following lemma states some basic properties of the scope function in λ-ap-ho-term-graphs.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
and let
\mathcal{G}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r,{\mathrm{P}}}}}\right\rangle
be a λ-ap-ho-term-graph over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
. Then the following statements hold:

(i)

Suppose that, for some
v,w∈V
,
v
occurs in
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)
. Then
v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)
, occurs in
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)
only once, and every access path of
w
passes through
v
, but does not end there, and thus
w≠v
. Furthermore it holds that
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)\,v≤{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)
. And conversely, if
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)=p\,v\,q
for some
p,q∈V^{*}
, then
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)=p
.

(ii)

Vertices in abstraction prefixes are abstraction vertices, and hence
{\mathrm{P}}
is of the form
{\mathrm{P}}:V\textrightarrow({\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right))^{*}
.

(iii)

For all
v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)
it holds that
v∉{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)
.

(iv)

While access paths might end in vertices in
{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{0}}}\right)
, they pass only through vertices in
{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)∪{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(@}}\right)
.

Proof.

Let
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
and let
\mathcal{G}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r,{\mathrm{P}}}}}\right\rangle
be a λ-ap-ho-term-graph over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
.

For showing
item

i
, let
v,w∈V
be such that
v
occurs in
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)
. Suppose further that
\pi
is an access path of
w
. Note that when walking through
\pi
the abstraction prefix starts out empty (due to
eq.

root
), and is expanded only in steps from vertices
v^{\prime}∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)
(due to
eq.

λ
,
eq.

@
, and
eq.

0
1

) in which just
v^{\prime}
is added to the prefix on the right (due to
eq.

λ
). Since
v
occurs in
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)
, it follows that
v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)
, that
v
must be visited on
\pi
, and that
\pi
continues after the visit to
v
. That
\pi
is an access path also entails that
v
is not visited again on
\pi
, hence that
w≠v
and that
v
occurs only once in
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)
, and that
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)\,v
, the abstraction prefix of the successor vertex of
v
on
\pi
, is a prefix of the abstraction prefix of every vertex that is visited on
\pi
after
v
.

Statements
item

ii
and
item

iii
follow directly from statement
item

i
.

For showing
item

iv
, consider an access path
\pi:r=w_{0}\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}\dots\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}w_{n}
that leads to a vertex
w_{n}∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{0}}}\right)
. If
i=0
, then there is no path that extends
\pi
properly beyond
w_{n}
. So suppose
i=1
, and let
w_{n+1}∈V
be such that
w_{n}\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}_{0}w_{n+1}
. Then
eq.

0
1

implies that
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w_{n}}}\right)={\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w_{n+1}}}\right)\,w_{n+1}
, from which it follows by
item

i
that
w_{n+1}
is visited already on
\pi
. Hence
\pi
does not extend to a longer path that is again an access path.
∎

In the following, let
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
and let
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{2}
be λ-ap-ho-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
with
\mathcal{G}_{k}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V_{k},{\mathrm{lab}}_{k},{\mathrm{args}}_{k},r_{k},{\mathrm{P}}_{k}}}}\right\rangle
for
k∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{1,2}}}\right\}
.

Definition 5.7

(homomorphism)
.

A
homomorphism
from
\mathcal{G}_{1}
to
\mathcal{G}_{2}
is a morphism from the structure
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V_{1},{\mathrm{lab}}_{1},{\mathrm{args}}_{1},r_{1},{\mathrm{P}}_{1}}}}\right\rangle
to the structure
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V_{2},{\mathrm{lab}}_{2},{\mathrm{args}}_{2},r_{2},{\mathrm{P}}_{2}}}}\right\rangle
, i.e. a function
h:V_{1}\textrightarrow V_{2}
such that
h
is a homomorphism from
G_{1}
to
G_{2}
, the term graphs underlying
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{2}
respectively, and additionally, for all
v∈V_{1}

h^{*}({\mathrm{P}}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right))={\mathrm{P}}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(h(v)}}\right)

where
{h^{*}}
is the homomorphic extension of
h
to words over
V_{1}
.

We then write
\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{h}\mathcal{G}_{2}
, or
\mathcal{G}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\leftarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{h}\mathcal{G}_{1}
. And we write
\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}\mathcal{G}_{2}
, or for that matter
\mathcal{G}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\leftarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}\mathcal{G}_{1}
, if there is a homomorphism from
\mathcal{G}_{1}
to
\mathcal{G}_{2}
.

§ 5.8

(isomorphism, bisimulation)
.

Analogous to
definition

4.11
and
definition

4.12
.
We denote by
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}}}_{{0}}^{\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}}}_{{1}}^{\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}
the isomorphism equivalence classes of λ-ho-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{0}}
and
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{1}}
, respectively.

§ 5.9

(relating λ-ho-term-graphs and λ-ap-ho-term-graphs)
.

The following proposition defines mappings between λ-ho-term-graphs and λ-ap-ho-term-graphs by which we establish a bijective correspondence between the two classes. For both directions the underlying λ-term-graph remains unchanged.
{\mathrm{A}}_{i}
derives an abstraction-prefix function
{\mathrm{P}}
from a scope function by assigning to each vertex a word of its binders in the correct nesting order.
{\mathrm{B}}_{i}
defines its scope function
{\mathrm{Sc}}
by assigning to each λ-vertex
v
the set of vertices that have
v
in their prefix (along with
v
since a vertex never has itself in its abstraction prefix).

Proposition 5.10

(relating λ-ho-term-graphs and λ-ap-ho-term-graphs)
.

For each
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
, the mappings
{\mathrm{A}}_{i}
and
{\mathrm{B}}_{i}
are well-defined between the class of λ-ho-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
and the class of λ-ap-ho-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{A}}_{i}:\penalty\

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{λ}_{i}\textrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\smash{(λ)}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r,{\mathrm{Sc}}}}}\right\rangle↦{\mathrm{A}}_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}}}\right):=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r,{\mathrm{P}}}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 8.61108pt\text{where }\begin{aligned} &{\mathrm{P}}:V\textrightarrow V^{*}\\
&v↦v_{0}\dots v_{n}\end{aligned}\text{ \penalty\ with\penalty\ }\begin{aligned} &{\mathrm{bds}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)\setminus\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{v}}}\right\}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{v_{0},\dots,v_{n}}}}\right\}\text{ and}\\
&{\mathrm{Sc}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v_{n}}}\right)\subset{\mathrm{Sc}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v_{n-1}}}\right)\dots\subset{\mathrm{Sc}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v_{0}}}\right)\end{aligned}

\displaystyle{\mathrm{B}}_{i}:\penalty\

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\smash{(λ)}}\textrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{λ}_{i}

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r,{\mathrm{P}}}}}\right\rangle↦{\mathrm{A}}_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}}}\right):=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r,{\mathrm{Sc}}}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 8.61108pt\text{where }\begin{aligned} {\mathrm{Sc}}:\penalty\ &{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)\textrightarrow\powerset(V)\\
&v↦\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{w∈V}\penalty\ \middle|\penalty\ {v\text{ occurs in }{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)}}}\right\}∪\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{v}}}\right\}\end{aligned}

On the isomorphism equivalence classes
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}}}^{\boldsymbol{λ}}_{i}
of
\mathcal{H}^{λ}_{i}
, and
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}}}_{{i}}^{\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}
of
\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\smash{(λ)}}
, the functions
{\mathrm{A}}_{i}
and
{\mathrm{B}}_{i}
induce the functions
{\textit{{A}}}_{i}:{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}}}^{\boldsymbol{λ}}_{i}\textrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}}}_{{i}}^{\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}
,
{[{\mathcal{G}}]}_{\mathrel{\sim}}↦{[{{\mathrm{A}}_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}}}\right)}]}_{\mathrel{\sim}}
and
{\textit{{B}}}_{i}:{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}}}_{{i}}^{\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\textrightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{H}}}^{\boldsymbol{λ}}_{i}
,
{[{\mathcal{G}}]}_{\mathrel{\sim}}↦{[{{\mathrm{B}}_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}}}\right)}]}_{\mathrel{\sim}}
.

Theorem 5.11

(correspondence between λ-ho-term-graphs and λ-ap-ho-term-graphs)
.

For each
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
it holds that the mappings
{\mathrm{A}}_{i}
and
{\mathrm{B}}_{i}
as defined in
proposition

5.10
are each other’s inverse; thus they define a bijective correspondence between the class of λ-ho-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
and the class of λ-ap-ho-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
. Furthermore, they preserve and reflect the sharing orders on
\mathcal{H}^{λ}_{i}
and on
\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\smash{(λ)}}
:

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\penalty\ {\mathrm{A}}_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}_{1}}}\right)\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}{\mathrm{A}}_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}_{1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}\mathcal{G}_{2}\penalty\

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\penalty\ \mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}\mathcal{G}_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathrm{B}}_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}_{1}}}\right)\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}{\mathrm{B}}_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}_{1}}}\right)\penalty\

.

The λ-ho-term-graphs in

{\mathrm{A}}_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}_{0}}}\right)=\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\prime}

{\mathrm{A}}_{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}_{1}}}\right)=\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime}

§ 5.13

(higher-order term graphs)
.

Due to this correspondence will henceforth sometimes say ‘higher-order term graph’ to refer to either λ-ho-term-graphs or λ-ap-ho-term-graphs.

6
λ-Term-Graphs without Scope Delimitiers

§ 6.1

(Overview)
.

In this section we examine (and dismiss) a naive approach to implement of functional bisimulation on higher-order term graphs as functional bisimulation on their underlying term graph, hoping that this application extends to the higher-order term graph without further ado. We demonstrate that this approach fails, concluding that a faithful first-order implementation of functional bisimulation must not neglect the scoping information that the higher-order term graphs carry.

§ 6.2

(variable backlinks)
.

For higher-order term graphs over the signature
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{0}}
(i.e. without variable backlinks) essential binding information is lost when looking only at the underlying term graph, to the extent that λ-terms cannot be unambiguously represented anymore. For instance the higher-order term graphs that represent the λ-terms
λ{xy}.\,{{x}\;{y}}
and
λ{xy}.\,{{x}\;{x}}
have the same underlying term graph.

This is different for higher-order term graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{1}}
, because the abstraction vertex to which a variable vertex belongs is uniquely identified by the variable backlink. This is why in this section we only consider term graphs and higher-order term graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{1}}
.

Definition 6.3

(λ-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{1}}
)
.

A term graph over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{1}}
is called a
λ-term-graph
over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{1}}
if it admits a correct abstraction-prefix function (or equivalently a correct scope function). By
\mathcal{T}_{1}^{λ}
we denote the class of λ-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{1}}
.

Definition 6.4

(functional bisimulation on the underlying term graph)
.

Let
G
be a higher-order term graph over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
for
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
with underlying term graph
G
. And suppose that there is a homomorphism
h
from
G
to
G^{\prime}
for some term graph
G^{\prime}
over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
.

We say that
h

extends to a homomorphism on

G
if there is a higher-order term graph
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i}}
which has
G^{\prime}
as its underlying term graph and
h
is a homomorphism from
G
to
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

We say that a class
K
of higher-order term graphs is
closed under functional bisimulation on the underlying term graphs
if for every
\mathcal{G}∈\mathcal{K}
with underlying term graph
G
, every homomorphism
h
on
G
extends to a homomorphism on
G
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Neither the class
\mathcal{H}^{λ}_{1}
of λ-ho-term-graphs nor the class
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\smash{(λ)}}
of λ-ap-ho-term-graphs is closed under functional bisimulation on the underlying term graphs.

Proof.

In view of
theorem

5.11
it suffices to show the statement for one of the two classes, say
\mathcal{H}^{λ}_{1}
. We show by example that not every homomorphism on the term graph underlying a λ-ho-term-graph over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{1}}
extends to a homomorphism on
\mathcal{H}^{λ}_{1}
.

Consider the λ-ho-term-graphs
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{0}
in
fig.

3
and their respective underlying term graphs
G_{1}
and
G_{0}
.

\mathcal{G}-1
:

\mathcal{G}-0
:

G-1
:

G-0
:

Figure 3:
There is no homomorphism between the λ-ho-term-graphs
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{G}_{0}
because of the scoping condition in
definition

4.10

eq.

. There is a homomorphism between their underlying term graphs
G_{1}
and
G_{0}
but it does not extend to
\mathcal{G}_{1}
.

There is an homomorphism

from

to
G_{0}
. However,
h
does not extend to a homomorphism on
\mathcal{G}_{1}
, since there is no homomorphism from
\mathcal{G}_{1}
to
\mathcal{G}_{0}
, and
\mathcal{G}_{0}
is the only λ-ho-term-graph with
G_{0}
as its underlying term graph (
G_{0}
admits only one scope function).
∎

The next proposition is merely a reformulation of
proposition

6.5
.

Proposition 6.6
.

The scope-forgetful mapping
{\mathrm{UL}}
, that maps higher-order term graphs to their underlying term graphs, preserves but does not reflect the sharing orders on the classes
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\smash{(λ)}}
and
\mathcal{H}^{λ}_{1}
. In particular for
\mathcal{K}∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\smash{(λ)}},\mathcal{H}^{λ}_{1}}}}\right\}
and
{\mathrm{UL}}{}:\mathcal{K}\textrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
it holds:

\displaystyle∀\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2}∈\mathcal{K}\penalty\

\displaystyle\penalty\ ⇒\penalty\ {\mathrm{UL}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}_{1}}}\right)\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}{\mathrm{UL}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}_{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}\mathcal{G}_{2}

\displaystyle\neg\forall\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2}∈\mathcal{K}\penalty\

\displaystyle\penalty\ ⇒\penalty\ \mathcal{G}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}\mathcal{G}_{2}

\displaystyle{\mathrm{UL}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}_{1}}}\right)\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}{\mathrm{UL}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{G}_{2}}}\right)

§ 6.7

(conclusion)
.

As a consequence of this proposition it is not possible to faithfully implement functional bisimulation on higher-order term graphs by only considering their underlying term graphs. It seems that we cannot simply discard the scoping information that is present in the higher-order term graphs. In the next section we refine the above approach by relying on a class of first-order term graphs that accounts for scoping by means of scope delimiter vertices.

7
λ-Term-Graphs with Scope Delimiters

§ 7.1

(overview)
.

Considering
6.7
we now enrich the signature for λ-term-graphs by an scope-delimiter symbol
\mathsf{S}
(cf.
\mathsf{S}
-steps of the decomposition systems). Delimiter vertices signify the end of a scope. We define variations of that signature where scope-delimiter vertices and/or variable vertices can have backlinks.

§ 7.2

(signature for λ-term-graphs with scope delimiters)
.

For all
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
and
j∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{1,2}}}\right\}
we define the signature
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}:=\Sigma^{λ}∪\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{\mathsf{0},\mathsf{S}}}}\right\}
as an extension of the signature
\Sigma^{λ}
where
{\mathrm{ar}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{0}}}\right)=i
and
{\mathrm{ar}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{S}}}\right)=j
, and we denote by
{\mathcal{T}}_{{i},{j}}:={\mathrm{TG}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}}}\right)
the class of term graphs over signature
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
.

§ 7.3

(variable and scope-delimiter backlinks)
.

Analogous to the classes
\mathcal{H}^{λ}_{i}
and
\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\smash{(λ)}}
, the index
i
determines whether variable vertices have backlinks to their corresponding abstraction. Here, the additional index
i
determines whether scope-delimiter vertices have such backlinks (if
j=2
) or not (if
j=1
).

§ 7.4

(relating scope delimiters and scope)
.

As the scope-delimiter vertices are meant to reproduce the scoping information in the higher-order term graphs, in order to relate the placements of
\mathsf{S}
-vertices and the scoping information we formulate abstraction-prefix conditions for λ-term-graphs (even though they do not carry abstraction prefixes). These conditions are based on
definition

5.4
and adapted to account for
\mathsf{S}
-vertices which are to decrease the abstraction prefix by one variable.

Definition 7.5

(abstraction-prefix function for
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
-term-graphs)
.

Let
G=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r}}}\right\rangle
be a
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
-term-graph for an
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
and an
j∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{1,2}}}\right\}
. A function
{\mathrm{P}}:V\textrightarrow V^{*}
from vertices of
G
to words of vertices is called an
abstraction-prefix function
for
G
. Such a function is called
correct
if for all
v,w∈V
and
k∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
the following holds:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(r}}\right)=\epsilon

(root)

\displaystyle v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ ∧\penalty\ v\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}_{0}w

\displaystyle⇒\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)={\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)\,v

(λ)

\displaystyle v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(@}}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ ∧\penalty\ v\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}_{k}w

\displaystyle⇒\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)={\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)

(@)

\displaystyle v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{0}}}\right)

\displaystyle⇒\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)≠\epsilon

(
\mathsf{0}_{0}
)

\displaystyle v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{0}}}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ ∧\penalty\ v\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}_{0}w

\displaystyle⇒\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle w∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)\penalty\ ∧\penalty\ {\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)\,w={\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)

(
\mathsf{0}_{1}
)

\displaystyle v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{S}}}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ ∧\penalty\ v\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}_{0}w

\displaystyle⇒\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)\,u={\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)\penalty\ \text{for some $u∈V$ }

(
\mathsf{S}_{0}
)

\displaystyle v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{S}}}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\ ∧\penalty\ v\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}_{1}w

\displaystyle⇒\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\

\displaystyle w∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)\penalty\ ∧\penalty\ {\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)\,w={\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)

(
\mathsf{S}_{1}
)

Note that analogous to
definition

4.6
and
definition

, if
i=0
, then

0

is trivially true, and if

then

is redundant, because it follows from

. Additionally, if

, then

S

is trivially true.

Definition 7.6

(λ-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
)
.

Let
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
and
j∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{1,2}}}\right\}
. A
λ-term-graph
over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
is a
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
-term-graph that admits a correct abstraction-prefix function. The class of λ-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
is denoted by
\mathcal{T}_{i,j}^{λ}
, and the class of isomorphism equivalence classes of λ-term-graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
by
{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{T}}}_{{i},{j}}^{\smash{\boldsymbol{(λ)}}}\!
.

Example 7.7
.

See

for examples, that, as we will see, correspond to the ho-term-graphs in

and in

. A precise formulation of this correspondence is given in

.

G-0
:

G-1
:

Figure 4:
The λ-term-graphs corresponding to the λ-ap-ho-term-graphs from

and the λ-ho-term-graphs from

.

The following lemma states some basic properties of λ-term-graphs.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
, and
j∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{1,2}}}\right\}
. Let
G=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V,{\mathrm{lab}},{\mathrm{args}},r}}}\right\rangle
be a λ-term-graph over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
, and let
{\mathrm{P}}
be a correct abstraction-prefix function for
G
. Then the statements
item

i
–
item

iii
from
lemma

5.6
hold as items (i)–(iii) of this lemma, and additionally:

(iv)

When stepping through
G
along a path,
{\mathrm{P}}
behaves like a stack data structure, in the sense that in a step
v\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}w
it holds:

∘

if
v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)
, then
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)={\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)\,v
, that is,
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)
is obtained by adding
v
to
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)
on the right (
v
is ‘pushed on the stack’);

∘

if
v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{0}}}\right)∪{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{S}}}\right)
, then
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)\,v={\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)
for some
v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)
, that is,
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)
is obtained by removing the rightmost vertex
v
from
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)
(
v
is ‘popped from the stack’);

∘

if
v∈{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(@}}\right)
, then
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w}}\right)={\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)
, i.e. the abstraction prefix remains the same.

(v)

Access paths may end in vertices in
{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{0}}}\right)
, but only pass through vertices in
{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(λ}}\right)∪{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(@}}\right)∪{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{S}}}\right)
, and depart from vertices in
{\mathrm{V}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathsf{S}}}\right)
only via indexed edges
\mathrel{\prescript{\smash{\mathsf{S}}}{}{\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}}}_{0}
.

(vi)

There is precisely one correct abstraction-prefix function for
G
.

Proof.

That also here
item

i
–
item

iii
from
lemma

5.6
hold, and that
item

v
holds, can be shown analogous to the proof of the respective items of
lemma

5.6
. Statement
item

iv
is easy to check from the definition (see
definition

7.5
of a correct abstraction-prefix function for a λ-term-graph). For
item

vi
it suffices to observe that if
{\mathrm{P}}
is a correct abstraction-prefix function for
G
, then, for all
v∈V
, the value
{\mathrm{P}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v}}\right)
of
{\mathrm{P}}
at
v
can be computed by choosing an arbitrary access path
\pi
from
r
to
v
and using the conditions
eq.

λ
,
eq.

@
, and
eq.

S
0

to determine in a stepwise manner the values of
{\mathrm{P}}
at the vertices that are visited on
\pi
. Hereby note that in every transition along an edge on
\pi
the length of the abstraction prefix only changes by at most 1.
∎

Terminology 7.9
.

Lemma

7.8

item

vi
allows us to speak of
the
abstraction-prefix function of a λ-term-graph.

§ 7.10

(λ-term-graphs are first-order)
.

Although the requirement of the existence of a correct abstraction-prefix function restricts their possible forms, λ-term-graphs are first-order term graphs, and as such the definitions of homomorphism, isomorphism, and bisimulation for first-order term graphs from
section

2
apply to them.

§ 7.11
.

The question arises now how a homomorphism
h
between λ-term-graphs
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
relates their abstraction-prefix functions
{\mathrm{P}}_{1}
and
{\mathrm{P}}_{2}
. As it turns out (
proposition

7.15
)
{\mathrm{P}}_{2}
is the ‘homomorphic image’ of
{\mathrm{P}}_{1}
under
h
in the sense of the following definition.

Definition 7.12

(homomorphic image)
.

Let
G_{1}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V_{1},{\mathrm{lab}}_{1},{\mathrm{args}}_{1},r_{1}}}}\right\rangle
and
G_{2}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V_{2},{\mathrm{lab}}_{2},{\mathrm{args}}_{2},r_{2}}}}\right\rangle
be term graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
for some
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
and
j∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{1,2}}}\right\}
, and let
{\mathrm{P}}_{1}
and
{\mathrm{P}}_{2}
be abstraction-prefix functions (not necessarily correct) for
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
, respectively. Furthermore, let
h:V_{1}\textrightarrow V_{2}
be a homomorphism from
G_{1}
to
G_{2}
.
We say that
{\mathrm{P}}_{2}
is the
homomorphic image of

{\mathrm{P}}_{1}

under

h
if it holds:

{h^{*}}∘{{\mathrm{P}}_{1}}={{\mathrm{P}}_{2}}∘{h}

(2)

where
h^{*}
is the homomorphic extension of
h
to words over
V_{1}
.

§ 7.13

(homomorphisms between λ-ap-ho-term-graphs)
.

Note that for λ-ap-ho-term-graphs
eq.

2
(or rather an equivalent thereof) holds by definition (see
definition

5.7
). This is a consequence of the fact that homomorphisms between λ-ap-ho-term-graphs are defined as morphisms between λ-ap-ho-term-graphs when viewed as algebraical structures. As the abstraction-prefix function is a part of λ-ap-ho-term-graphs it has to be respected by morphisms.

§ 7.14

(homomorphisms between λ-term-graphs)
.

λ-term-graphs however, do not include an abstraction-prefix function as part of their formalisation. Here the existence of an abstraction-prefix function is merely a mathematical property that distinguishes them from among the term graphs over the same signature. Homomorphisms between λ-term-graphs are defined as morphisms between the structures that underlie their formalisation, i.e. first-order term graphs, and therefore do not by definition respect abstraction-prefix functions.

However, it turns out that, that due the correctness conditions for λ-term-graphs, homomorphisms between λ-term-graphs do in fact respect their abstraction-prefix function:

Proposition 7.15
.

Let
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
be λ-term-graphs, and let
{\mathrm{P}}_{1}
and
{\mathrm{P}}_{2}
be their abstraction-prefix functions, respectively. Suppose that
h
is a homomorphism from
G_{1}
to
G_{2}
. Then
{\mathrm{P}}_{2}
is the homomorphic image of
{\mathrm{P}}_{1}
under
h
.

This proposition follows from statement
item

i
of the following lemma.
Item

ii
states that functional bisimulation on term graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
preserves and reflects correctness of the abstraction-prefix functions.

Lemma 7.16
.

Let
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
be term graphs over
\Sigma^{λ}_{\mathsf{0}_{i},\mathsf{S}_{j}}
for some
i∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{0,1}}}\right\}
and
j∈\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{{1,2}}}\right\}
. Let
h
be a homomorphism from
G_{1}
to
G_{2}
. Furthermore, let
{\mathrm{P}}_{1}
and
{\mathrm{P}}_{2}
be their abstraction-prefix functions, respectively. Then the following two statements hold:

(i)

If
{\mathrm{P}}_{1}
and
{\mathrm{P}}_{2}
are correct for
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
, respectively, then
{\mathrm{P}}_{2}
is the homomorphic image of
{\mathrm{P}}_{1}
.

(ii)

If
{\mathrm{P}}_{2}
is the homomorphic image of
{\mathrm{P}}_{1}
, then
{\mathrm{P}}_{1}
is correct for
G_{1}
if and only if
{\mathrm{P}}_{2}
is correct for
G_{2}
.

Proof.

Let
G_{1}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V_{1},{\mathrm{lab}}_{1},{\mathrm{args}}_{1},r_{1}}}}\right\rangle
and
G_{2}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle{V_{2},{\mathrm{lab}}_{2},{\mathrm{args}}_{2},r_{2}}}}\right\rangle
.
In this proof we denote by
\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}^{\prime}
the directed-edge relation in
G_{2}
, thus if we for instance write
v\mathrel{\rightarrowtail}^{\prime}_{0}w
we mean to say that
w
is the first child of
v
in
G_{2}
.

For proving
item

i
, we assume that the abstraction-prefix functions

§ 7.17

(relating λ-term-graphs defined above and λ-ap-ho-term-graphs)
.

We now proceed to define a precise relationship between λ-term-graphs and λ-ap-ho-term-graphs via mappings that translate between these classes:

The mapping
{\mathrm{G}}_{i,j}

produces a λ-term-graph for any given λ-ap-ho-term-graph by adding to the original set of vertices a number of delimiter vertices at the appropriate places. That is, at every position where the abstraction prefix decreases by
n
elements,
n

\mathsf{S}
-vertices are inserted as depicted in
fig.

5
. In the image, the original abstraction prefix is retained as part of the vertices, which we use for defining the edges of the image.

The mapping
{\mathrm{\mathcal{G}}}_{i,j}

back to λ-ap-ho-term-graphs is simpler because it only has to erase the
\mathsf{S}
-vertices, and add the correct abstraction prefix that we already know to exists for the λ-term-graph.

§ 7.24

(a weaker than bijective correspondence)
.
